# Lessons from graded ad hoc calls

Read before every call. Each entry: what decided the move, what the call missed, and what data
would have priced it. Each lesson is a hypothesis with a running record (helped / hurt / neutral
on later calls where it applied); none has a record yet.

- 2026-09-29 JEF (UP 3.7 %, realised -1.29 %, miss): the UP mechanism fired (investment banking
  and advisory beat) and the stock still fell. A reaction miss: a beat that the market had already
  priced. Next time: measure how much the stock ran into the print and how often its beats were sold.
- 2026-09-29 CCL (DOWN 5.7 %, realised +13.42 %, miss, outside the band): three of four UP triggers
  fired (adjusted EPS beat, FY guide raised, yields up). The call leaned on the stock's own history.
  Next time: put a probability on the guide-raise trigger from the company's guidance history.
- 2026-10-01 MU (DOWN 6.8 %, realised +3.03 %, miss): the DOWN triggers on the margin guide and capex
  fired, but the revenue and EPS guide beat what the Street needed by 6-7 %, and that dominated. The
  DOWN trigger needed one leg ("or") while the UP trigger needed all three ("and"): lopsided design.
  Next time: weight the primary metric's beat size against secondary misses; keep triggers symmetric.
- 2026-10-01 ACN (DOWN 6.0 %, realised +15.78 %, miss, far outside the band): 9 of 13 UP triggers
  fired; the currency premise behind the DOWN leg was wrong (the company guided FX flat), and the
  sector-wide "AI shrinks consulting" fear was already priced (stock down about a third on the year).
  Next time: check the company's own guidance conventions before using them as a trigger, and treat
  a crowded sector fear as a source of upside when the print disproves it.
- 2026-10-02 NKE (DOWN 8.0 %, realised about -3.6 % provisional, HIT, size 4.4 points too big): the
  8-K carried items 2.02 and 2.05 (restructuring costs). The mechanism is still to be read when the
  repository is restored.
- General: the stock's own up/down record carried no direction information (0.501 on 976 held-out
  prints); when research leaned, it leaned DOWN (5 of 5 calls before NKE), and the six DOWN calls
  before NKE all rose. Watch for a one-way lean.
- 2026-10-06 2317.TW Foxconn Sept revenue (UP 1.2 %, realised +0.79 % provisional, HIT, inside the
  band): the print landed in the top scenario (first NT$1 tn month, Q3 +20 % QoQ), the stock gapped
  +3.5 % and faded to +0.8 %. Hypothesis for the monthly-revenue class: a strong headline is mostly
  absorbed at the open and the close keeps a fraction of it. Compute next time: the gap-retention
  ratio (close-to-close over close-to-open) on past revenue days, and discount a pre-open gap with it.
  Record: helped / hurt / neutral to be filled at the next monthly-revenue grade.
- 2026-10-06 RPM fiscal Q1 2027 (UP 1.5 %, realised +3.16 % official, HIT, 1.7 pp too small): the print
  was mixed (EPS beat at the guide's low end, FY27 EBITDA range reset from +5-10 % to mid-single-digit,
  Q2 guided low-to-mid single digit, CPG organic −1.7 %) and the stock still rose, on a +1.3 % SPY day,
  back to Monday's VWAP after a last-15-minute liquidation had set the reference close 2.9 % too low.
  Hypothesis 1: when the eve-of-print close is >2 % below that day's 10:00-15:45 VWAP with no filing and
  flat peers, the reversion is a directional input (P ~0.65, n = 1), not variance. Hypothesis 2: a
  multi-day drift cell must be conditioned on the shape of the slide; the "0 of 5 up after a −4 % drift"
  cell hurt here (it halved the size). Record: helped / hurt / neutral to be filled at the next print
  where either applies.
- 2026-10-07 STZ fiscal Q2 2027, post-release class (DOWN 4.5 % called at 4:50 pm with the stock
  −4.6 % after hours; realised +2.35 %, MISS, outside the band): the after-hours tape agreed with
  the release read (shipments 6 pts ahead of depletions, margin −160 bps, no raise), but the 8 am
  call added a current-month datum ("September depletions trending in the right direction") and a
  "top of the range" steer, and the stock, at a 52-week low on a sub-10× multiple, opened +0.6 %
  and closed +2.35 %. Hypothesis 1: an after-hours print does not measure the next close when a
  management call sits between it and the open; cap P(carry) ~0.6 and give the reversal ≥ 0.25
  when the base is de-rated and the negative is a detail management can reframe (STZ now 2 of 6
  such reversals). Hypothesis 2 (ACN/RPM, strengthened): from a de-rated base a beat with any
  forward positive is bought — RPM and STZ this week, 2 of 2; make it a named conditional, not a
  tail. Data: reference closes come from the settled historical row, not the day-of quote endpoint
  (second occurrence; 19 cents here). Record: helped / hurt / neutral to be filled at the next
  post-release or de-rated-base call.


## 2026-10-08 — Samsung (005930), pre-print data-source lesson (recorded before grading)
- **What happened:** two "official close" sources disagreed for Oct 7 (Naver mobile daily API 269,000 vs Daum/stockanalysis 268,500) and for Oct 6 (273,000 vs 272,000). Daum, stockanalysis and Yahoo agree with each other; Naver's mobile daily API reports the NXT-integrated (KRX+NXT) close since the alternative exchange launched. The call document had briefly treated the Naver figure as the KRX close.
- **Rule (hypothesis, status: new):** for Korean names the grading reference is the KRX regular-session close from the Daum days API, cross-checked with stockanalysis; the Naver mobile daily API is a context source only. Flag any Korean close that is quoted from a single source as provisional. Same shape as the Nasdaq "Closed at 4:00 PM vs settled row" lesson: the first number a convenient endpoint gives is not the settled reference.
- **How to compute next time:** pull Daum days API (symbolCode=A<code>) and stockanalysis krx-<code> on the evening before the print, record both; if they differ by more than one tick, hold the reference as provisional and resolve from the KRX MI_INDEX-equivalent (data.krx.co.kr) before grading.


## 2026-10-08 (graded 2026-10-09 04:50 JST): Samsung, Quanta, and a missed call

- **Samsung, closing-price sources disagree (measured).** Daum (KRX) 262,000; stockanalysis 264,500; Naver 263,000. The earlier rule "Daum and stockanalysis agree, Naver is NXT-integrated" failed on Oct 8. Hypothesis: stockanalysis includes NXT after-hours trades. Rule to test: a KRX close must sit inside the day's low-high range, and the low must not exceed the close. Compute next time: pull the KRX-only source and the NXT-integrated source the evening before the print, and record both.
- **Samsung, fundamentals unscored.** The DART viewer was down for maintenance and the figures were not retrieved, so KS1 to KS3 stayed unscored. Hypothesis: the direction came from the index (KOSPI -2.62%), not the print. Compute next time: keep a second route to the disclosed figures, and mark unscored fundamentals as unscored.
- **Quanta, locked call against the record's own rule (measured).** The document flips the side to DOWN if KS1 does not fire; KS1 did not fire; the locked line stayed UP and missed (-2.84%). Hypothesis: the lock was applied as a rule, not as a decision the document explicitly reopened. Compute next time: write one governing rule before the call, and state in the call which rule decides the side.
- **TSMC call missed (measured).** The ad hoc TSMC call was scheduled for 01:30 UTC Oct 8. No call document exists. The wake sat in the queue while the session was idle and was read only at 19:17 UTC, after the 05:30 UTC release. No pre-release call was made, and none is back-dated here. Process lesson: a wake that fires while the session is idle needs a pre-release action that does not depend on a live turn, or the call should be made in advance and locked, not left to the wake.
- **Fast Retailing, pending.** Call DOWN 5.5%, made 00:20 UTC before the 15:31 JST release. The release figures were not verifiable from this container. Reference close 74,800; grade Fri Oct 9 close against it.
- **Foxconn, verified.** Settled TPE rows: Oct 5 close 254.00, Oct 6 open 263.00 (+3.54%), close 256.00 (+0.79%); close 2.66 pp below the open. The doc's figures for the open, close and fade match. The September revenue figure (NT$1,158,633 million) was not re-read in this check.

## 2026-10-08 — LEVI, APLD, PEP (three pre-release calls graded the same evening; closes provisional)

All Oct 8 closes below are the Nasdaq quote endpoint's 4 pm values (M, scratchpad/grade_1008/FACTS_1008.md); the settled rows were not posted at 20:36 UTC and can differ by 0.1-0.2 % (RPM, STZ precedent). Confirm wake Oct 9 00:40 UTC. LEVI's and PEP's directions cannot flip on that drift; APLD's can.

- **Outcomes (M closes; D arithmetic).**
  - LEVI: called DOWN 6.5 %, P(up) 0.48; 19.51 → 19.04 = −2.41 %; **HIT** (provisional); Brier 0.230; coin DOWN, hit. Scenario A on the letter (raise + $0.48), sold.
  - APLD: called UP 8.0 %, P(up) 0.51; 23.81 → 23.85 = +0.17 %; **provisional HIT** — flips to MISS if the settled close is at or below 23.81; Brier 0.240 (0.260 on a flip); coin DOWN, provisional miss (hit on a flip). Scenario B, flat against a group down 3.64-7.55 % (median −6.5 %).
  - PEP: called DOWN 3.0 %, P(up) 0.48; 123.73 → 128.34 = +3.73 %; **MISS**; Brier 0.270; coin UP, hit. Scenario C (EPS guide cut) with two operating UP legs fired, bought.

- **Prior lessons as hypotheses, marks pooled across the three names (D from the three resolutions).**
  - CCL (probability on the guide change from the company's own history): LEVI helped (P(raise) 0.53, the raise came), APLD neutral (dates reaffirmed, run-rate not in the exhibit), PEP hurt ("EPS cuts only at Feb/Apr" refuted by the exhibit). Pooled 1 helped / 1 hurt / 1 neutral. Companion rule needed: a P(guide change) is only useful with a P(up | that change) that is re-derived from evidence, not carried from a prior.
  - ACN (check guidance conventions; crowded fear is upside when the print disproves it): LEVI helped (FX convention flagged; the H2 fear was not disproved and had not been pre-paid), APLD helped (MW-to-NOI fear disproved, paid +2.6 to +5.7 relative), PEP helped (NA volume fear partly disproved, that was the upside). Pooled 3 helped / 0 / 0. Strongest record so far.
  - RPM H1 (eve close > 2 % below the 10:00-15:45 mean → reversion input): not triggered in any of the three (LEVI −1.0 %, APLD −0.67 %, PEP −0.5 %); none reverted. Pooled 0 / 0 / 3 neutral. No evidence either way; the rule's 2 % gate behaved.
  - RPM H2 (condition drift cells on the shape of the slide): LEVI helped (decomposing the October cell surfaced the "raise with next quarter below the Street" shape that printed), APLD helped (sector-shaped drift treated as context, no DOWN shave), PEP hurt (single-name selling carried as a "mild negative" decided the side; the d21 ≤ −5 cell was right again, now 6/6). Pooled 2 helped / 1 hurt / 0.
  - RPM lesson 3 (magnitude = expected absolute move E|r|): LEVI hurt (6.5 vs 2.41), APLD hurt (8.0 vs 0.17; the doc's size-test clause fired), PEP helped (3.0 vs 3.73). Pooled 1 helped / 2 hurt / 0. See the pattern below.
  - Foxconn (gap retention; record the open vs the reference): LEVI helped as a grading tool (0.71 × gap predicted 19.06 vs 19.04 realised), APLD neutral (gap inside the ±2 % cell; retention 0.15 recorded), PEP neutral (up-gap extended, retention 5.27; 19/20 same sign). Pooled 1 helped / 0 / 2 neutral. Not a directional input in any of the three.
  - STZ (an after-hours print is not the next close): LEVI helped (+6.5 % at 4:13 pm, −2.41 % at the close), APLD neutral (first AH print +1.1 %, close +0.17 %; not graded on it), PEP neutral (pre-release class). Pooled 1 helped / 0 / 2 neutral. The LEVI case is the second clean reversal (STZ, LEVI).
  - Also applied: JEF (run-in / de-rated base) LEVI hurt, APLD neutral, PEP helped = 1/1/1; MU (symmetric triggers, primary metric) LEVI neutral, APLD helped, PEP hurt = 1/1/1; NKE (8-K items) 3 neutral; one-way-lean check 1 helped (APLD) / 2 neutral.

- **Cross-name patterns (D unless marked).**
  - Every P(up) was within 0.03 of a coin: 0.48, 0.51, 0.48 (|0.48 − 0.50| = 0.02; |0.51 − 0.50| = 0.01). Evening Brier: 0.230 + 0.240 + 0.270 = 0.740 vs the coin's 0.750; on an APLD flip 0.760 vs 0.750. The research and the coin are 0.01-0.02 apart on three calls.
  - Sizes: called 6.5 / 8.0 / 3.0, realised 2.41 / 0.17 / 3.73. Errors −4.09, −7.83, +0.73 pp: two large overcalls, one undercall. Mean absolute size error 4.2 pp.
  - Scenario A was sold (LEVI: P(up|A) 0.70, realised −2.41 %), scenario C was bought (PEP: P(up|C) 0.10, realised +3.73 %), scenario B was flat against a peer group down 3.64-7.55 % (APLD: B |r| 4-5 % modelled, realised 0.17 %). In all three the scenario the doc filed the print under predicted the wrong sign or the wrong size, while the kill-switch tables correctly pre-named the legs that fired (LEVI KS3 low leg and KS4 DOWN leg; APLD KS1, KS2, KS6; PEP KS1 UP, KS2 UP, KS5 CUT). The research read the prints well and the reactions poorly.
  - The sector or index did a large share of two of the three moves (M closes from FACTS; D shares): PEP — XLP +2.11 of +3.73 = 57 % of the size (beta 1 to XLP assumed, H); APLD — group median −6.5 % × beta 0.85 = −5.5 pts of drag against a +0.17 % close, so the name-specific part (+5.7 at 4 pm; +2.6 to +3.8 through 3 pm) and the sector part cancelled; LEVI — XRT +1.21 % worked against the move (excess −3.62 pp), so the move was name-specific. SPY −0.39 %, QQQ −1.28 % on the day.
  - The magnitude anchor "expected absolute move" over-sizes when the modal scenario is the in-line one (H, 2 of 3): APLD's modal B (P 0.50) carried |r| 4-5 % but the mixture E|r| gave 8.0 against 0.17 realised; LEVI's E|r| 6.5 against 2.41 (the A down-size −4 or B's −5 would have been 1.6-2.6 pp off). PEP is the counterexample: E|r| 3.0 against 3.73, where a +2.11 % staples day added about 2 points to an in-line-sized name move (+1.6 pp vs XLP).

- **New lessons (each a testable rule, with the number to compute and what disproves it).**
  1. **Ex-item EPS bar (LEVI).** Before any print with a known one-off cash item pending, compute item_eps = item paid × (1 − guided tax rate) / diluted shares; LEVI: 80 × 0.77 / 390.6 M = $0.16 = 44 % of the $0.36 consensus (D, from the doc's 10-Q figures). If item_eps ≥ 25 % of consensus, define the beat switch and scenario A on adj EPS − gross item_eps, and map "FY raise ≤ net item" to the hold scenario. Here 0.48 − 0.16 = 0.32 < 0.36 → B → P(up) 0.27 (D), off the coin. Also read the company's own non-GAAP definition table: tariff refunds were never on LEVI's exclusion list (M, exhibit). Disproof: across the next five prints with a disclosed one-off ≥ 25 % of consensus, if the sign of (adj EPS − item_eps − consensus) predicts r no better than the headline beat sign.
  2. **Condition the raise scenario on the next-quarter implied guide (LEVI).** Compute next-quarter implied EPS = new FY top − year-to-date; LEVI: 1.56 − 0.70 − 0.48 = $0.38 vs the $0.48 Street (D). If it is below consensus, P(up | raise) ≤ 0.30 (cell now 0/3: Oct-25 −12.55, Jul-26 −2.17, Oct-26 −2.41). Disproof: a close-up print in that configuration (0/3 → 1/4) or two in five.
  3. **Size to the modal scenario's conditional |r| when P(modal) ≥ 0.50; keep the tails in the band (APLD, LEVI).** APLD: 4.5 % instead of 8.0 → error −4.3 pp instead of −7.8 (D). Test on the next five calls: the modal-conditional size must beat E|r| on at least 3 of 5. Disproof: E|r| wins 3 of 5. PEP's +0.73 pp is already one point against.
  4. **A relative line for high-beta names (APLD).** When the 63-day beta to the peer basket is ≥ 0.8 and the basket sd ≥ 4 %/day, grade r_rel = r − β × basket as a second line. Fix the basket (the doc's five names, equal weight) and the statistic before the print; pull all five at the close; record r_rel at 3:00 pm and 4:00 pm. Today: 3 pm +2.8, 4 pm +5.7 (D, chart files); the last hour alone added +2.9 pts on a flat group. Pass if |r_rel − template median| < |r − called size| on ≥ 3 of the next 5 high-beta in-line prints. Disproof: 2 or fewer of 5.
  5. **Split the cut scenario into cut-with-miss and cut-with-beat (PEP).** C-beat = guide cut with the EPS UP leg or organic UP leg fired. Seed P(up | C-beat, de-rated base) at 0.50 from the doc's own cells (PEP cuts from within 3 % of the low 1/2: Apr-25 −4.89, Oct-26 +3.73; slide-side trims with EPS held 3/3 non-negative; same-window peer cuts 0/2 up, de-rated reaffirms with negative volumes 0/2 up). Size the up at A's up-size. Disproof: fewer than 3 of the next 5 beat-and-cut prints from a de-rated base (any name) close up.
  6. **Every scenario conditional goes in the flip table with its evidence cell and the stage that last touched it (PEP).** P(up|C) 0.10 was a stage-1 prior nobody re-derived while six other parameters moved; the side flipped at 0.18 (D). Rule: flag any conditional still at its stage-1 value; floor/cap at 0.20/0.80 when the cell has n < 8 unless it is the company's own same-configuration record. Test: re-run the last five N3 mixtures with the floor and count direction changes. Disproof: the floored mixtures miss more often than the originals over the next ten calls.
  7. **Eve-of-print single-name selling: a size input, not yet a direction input.** The evidence conflicts: LEVI's −5.0 % on 6.6× the 20-day average with no filing (D from the settled row: 19.51 / 20.53 − 1; 18.03 M / 2.72 M) preceded a revenue miss and a DTC shortfall, and the two-day move 20.53 → 19.04 = −7.26 % matched the 6.5 % called; PEP's −1.4 pt eve residual vs XLP preceded a +3.73 % close. Rule to test: when the eve move is ≤ −3 % on ≥ 2× ADV with peers within ±1 % and no filing, log both the one-day and the two-day |r| for the next five such prints, and carry the residual as a two-sided size amplifier. Disproof of the "informed" reading: 3 of 5 such prints close up.
  8. **Data rules (M, this evening).** The 1-minute chart's points after 4:00 pm are after-hours prints (LEVI 4:01-4:02, APLD 4:05, PEP 4:05), and the chart's 4:00 pm point can itself disagree with the quote endpoint (PEP 127.96 vs 128.34; APLD 23.84 vs 23.85). The quote endpoint's day range can disagree with the chart's regular session (LEVI 18.58-19.34 vs 18.65-19.13; APLD 22.62-24.42 vs 22.63-24.18; PEP 124.05-128.90 vs 124.41-128.62). The CBOE delayed quote is a usable cross-check when its last trade is 15:59:59 (APLD, PEP) and not when the feed is stale (LEVI, last trade 10:48). The settled row decides everything; grade on the quote endpoint, show the alternatives, re-stamp at the confirm wake.
  9. **Score the equity switch from the cash flow statement on the night (APLD).** Preferred proceeds ($275.0 M), non-cash conversions to common ($149.9 M) and the change in shares issued (+9.18 M, +3.1 %) were all in the EX-99.1 (M); the balance-sheet residual ($124.9 M) understated the issuance by 2.2× (D). Disproof: none needed; a reading rule.
  10. **8-K item numbers come from the 8-K cover or the EDGAR index, not the exhibit (PEP, LEVI).** The exhibit grep cannot show items 2.02/2.05; what it can show is restructuring inside core adjustments (PEP $192 M / $0.11 per share, M). Also: carry accession numbers from the index file, never retyped (LEVI's was transposed in the first grade).

- **Running tallies (M, running record; APLD provisional).**
  - Pre-release research calls: Foxconn HIT, RPM HIT, Samsung HIT, Quanta MISS, LEVI HIT, APLD provisional HIT, PEP MISS = 5 of 7 (4 of 7 if APLD's settled close is at or below 23.81). Post-release: STZ MISS. All graded calls 5 of 8 (4 of 8 on a flip).
  - Coin benchmark: Foxconn hit, RPM hit, STZ hit, Samsung hit, Quanta miss, LEVI hit, APLD provisional miss, PEP hit = 6 of 8 (7 of 8 on a flip).
  - This evening's Brier: research 0.740 (0.760 on a flip) vs coin 0.750 over three calls.
  - Directions this evening: 2 DOWN (LEVI hit, PEP miss), 1 UP (APLD provisional hit). No one-way lean.

- **Measured, derived, hypothesised.**
  - Measured (M): the Oct 7 settled closes; the Oct 8 quote-endpoint, chart, CBOE and app values; the 8-K acceptance stamps; every exhibit figure quoted above; the peer and index closes in FACTS.
  - Derived (D): every return, excess, Brier, share, implied-guide and ex-item figure, with the arithmetic shown in the three resolutions.
  - Hypothesised (H): the beta-1 sector attributions; the "over-sizes when modal is in-line" pattern (2 of 3); rules 1-7 above and their seeds; the APLD last-hour cause; the PEP day-5 give-back test.

- **Alternative interpretation (H).** With eight graded calls, all three of tonight's at P(up) within 0.03 of 0.50, research 5 of 8 against the coin's 6 of 8 is indistinguishable from chance: a fair coin lands 5 or more of 8 about 36 % of the time (D: (56 + 28 + 8 + 1) / 256 = 93 / 256). On this reading the hit rate carries no information yet; the kill-switch tables do (they pre-named the legs that fired in all three), and so do the lessons — which is why each is written as a rule with a disproof. A second alternative: two of the three moves were mostly the sector's (PEP 57 % XLP; APLD's −5.5 pts of group drag cancelled against the name), so the evening tested the sector more than the method, and only LEVI was a clean name-specific test — and that one the research got right by the mixture arithmetic while its modal conditional (P(up|A) 0.70) pointed the wrong way.

- **Settled-source check, 2026-10-09 00:42 UTC (M).** Nasdaq historical rows for 10/08 still unposted. stockanalysis daily rows and CBOE delayed quotes (last trade 15:59:59) agree: LEVI 19.05 (−2.36 %; the Nasdaq quote endpoint said 19.04, the app 19.03), APLD 23.85 (+0.17 %; three sources agree — the provisional HIT is corroborated, official stamp pending), PEP 128.34 (+3.73 %; three sources agree; the app's 128.64 is the outlier). No grade changes. Data rule 8 above gains a line: when the Nasdaq row lags, two settled-type sources (stockanalysis daily + CBOE with a 15:59:59 last trade) agreeing is the interim standard; a phone-app "at close" value is not.

## 2026-10-09 — Fast Retailing (9983), FY2026 results (pre-release call; Oct 9 close provisional)

**Outcome (M closes; D arithmetic):** called DOWN 5.5 %, P(up) 0.46; 74,800 → 71,780 = −4.04 %; **HIT** (provisional); Brier 0.2116 vs the coin's 0.250; coin DOWN, hit; size 1.46 pp too big; inside the −7 / +7.5 band.

- **Data status (M, scratchpad/fr_grade/FACTS_FR.md).** The Oct 9 close is the stockanalysis TYO daily row (fetched 31 minutes after the 15:30 JST close); the Yahoo Japan post-close quote page shows the same open, high, low, close and volume. The official TSE row was not read (kabutan blocked). The TDnet release stamp was not read. The official Nikkei 225 close was not retrieved; a 15:45 delayed quote after the close shows 69,030.92, −0.02 %. The QUICK FY2027 consensus (Nikkei, ~15:37 JST) was not retrieved. Confirm wake 12:00 UTC. The direction cannot flip on a tick; the size and Brier can move in the second decimal.

- **What printed (M = company results-summary page, saved; S = press copies of the tanshin; D = arithmetic).** FY2026 BP ¥718.4 bn vs the ¥710 bn plan (+1.18 %); OP ¥743.1 bn (S) vs 730 (+1.79 %); NP ¥542.5 bn vs 500 (+8.5 %); fifth record year. FY2027 BP ¥830.0 bn (+15.5 %), OP = BP (S), pre-tax ¥880.0 bn (+9.7 %), NP ¥560.0 bn (+3.2 %), DPS ¥900; FY2026 DPS raised to ¥850 (+¥210 vs the Jul plan). Against the Street (doc, S&P, 16 analysts): OP 830 vs 832.15 = −0.26 % (B−, ¥2.15 bn under); NP 560 vs 580.7 = −3.56 % (C); revenue +0.80 %; DPS +31 %. Tape: open +0.94 % (high at 09:00), low −4.25 % at 11:20, close −4.04 %; volume 2.30× Thursday's; the close is about ¥10 above the Oct 2 level (D: 74,690 / 1.0407 ≈ 71,770) — the September-sales jump and everything after it was given back.

- **Prior lessons as hypotheses, one mark each (D from the resolution).**
  - JEF (measure the run-in; pre-bought beats get sold): **helped**. d21 +9.97 %, d5 +5.53 % (doc) went into P(up|B−) 0.35 and the down sizes; the in-line guide was sold in the Jul-26 shape and the stock round-tripped to the Oct 2 level. In-line-after-run-in cell (Jul-26, Oct-26): 0 of 2 up.
  - CCL (probability on the guide trigger from the company's own history): **helped**. Centre 824, sd 30 from five initial BP-guide growth rates, OP = BP and the +1.3 pp FX rule; actual 830 (z 0.2); P(A) 0.13 and A did not happen; OP = BP printed again (S). This is the lesson that moved 0.56 to 0.46 and flipped the side. Not applied to the NP line, where the same conventions had already predicted the miss.
  - MU (symmetric triggers; weight the primary metric): **neutral**. The ±3 % legs on one metric mapped to exclusive scenarios summing to 1.00 and worked; but a single-metric table had no row for the NP line the market priced, and both switches that fired (KS4, KS5) were scored as positives.
  - ACN (check guidance conventions; crowded fear is upside when disproved): **helped**. OP = BP and the ¥50 bn finance-income line both printed (D: 880 − 830 = 50); no crowded short, so no upside credit was given to B−; the stock fell.
  - NKE (read the filing items): **neutral**. Not applicable to a tanshin; the Q4 FY2025 ¥3.0 bn impairment-reversal base effect sat inside KS3, which did not fire.
  - RPM H1 (eve close > 2 % below the 10:00-15:00 VWAP with a flat index = reversion input): **neutral**. Thursday's VWAP is not in FACTS; the close 74,800 was +0.03 % and 36 % up its day range (D), so the trigger is unlikely to have fired (H); no reversion followed. No evidence either way.
  - RPM H2 (condition drift cells on the shape of the slide): **neutral**. No drift or seasonal cell was used as a direction input. Nothing to score.
  - RPM lesson 3 (magnitude = E|r|, not the signed median): **neutral**. Called 5.5 vs 4.04. E|r| 4.85 alone would have been 0.81 pp off, the signed median −1.0 would have been 3.0 pp off, so the lesson pointed the right way; the blend toward the October median 6.1 added 0.65 pp. The modal B− down size −4 was 0.04 pp off: one point for LESSONS rule 3 (PEP's +0.73 is the one against).
  - STZ (after-hours print is not the close; from a de-rated base a beat with a forward positive is bought): **helped**. The base was judged only partly de-rated (−15 % from the high, +10 % in 21 sessions, ~39.5× forward; doc), the "bought" conditional was not triggered, and a record year with a dividend surprise was sold.
  - Foxconn (gap retention; discount a pre-open gap by the retention ratio): **neutral**. Recorded as not transferable to this name; no discount applied. The gap +0.94 % sat under the cell's 1 % filter and reversed (close/gap −4.3). Had the 1.82 / 17-of-18 extension been used, it would have pointed UP.
  - Samsung data-source (settled reference from the exchange row, not a quote endpoint): **helped**. Reference 74,800 from the settled row, not the 09:12 JST quote 75,410 (D: that would have read −4.81 %). Partial: the official TSE row is still unread.
  - One-way-lean check: **helped**. The skeptic's +0.01 lean correction and +0.02 ABC-Mart tape rule both pointed UP; shown as sensitivities (0.49, same side), not folded in (0.52 UP would have missed).

- **New lessons (each a testable rule, with the number to compute and what disproves it).**
  1. **The guide-distribution centre held; grade it as a number, not a side (CCL extension).** Centre 824, sd 30; realised 830 (z 0.2, D); P(G ≥ 832) 0.39 and the guide landed ¥2.15 bn under. Compute next time: the three builds (structural, momentum-capped, segment/margin), their centre and sd, and z = (G − centre) / sd at grading. Pass: |z| ≤ 1 on 4 of the next 5 prints with a guide. Disproof: |z| > 1 on 2 of 5, or 3 of 5 on the same sign (a biased centre).
  2. **A guided line below operating profit (NP, pre-tax, finance, tax) needs its own switch.** NP 560 vs 580.7 = −3.56 % (D) was a C-band miss while OP was B−; the doc's own dividend arithmetic had FY2027 NP at 545-565 (doc), −6.1 % to −2.7 % vs the Street (D), and never turned it into a switch. Compute next time: ±3 % bands on every line the company will guide (BP/OP, pre-tax, NP, EPS) against same-source Street numbers; EPS on the Street's own implied share count (580.7 / 1.891 = 307.1 m, D), never the total count. If the lines disagree, the scenario is the lower band, or at least P(up|B−) → 0.25 and the size → the lower band's down size. Here that gives P(up) 0.38 and a Brier of 0.14 (H mixture: 0.133 × 0.80 + 0.257 × 0.50 + 0.320 × 0.25 + 0.290 × 0.22). Disproof: on the next five prints where the lines disagree, the OP line alone predicts the sign at least as often as the lowest-band line.
  3. **A margin under 1 % of the bar is "in line", not a scenario.** The OP line crossed the B−/B+ boundary by 0.26 % on a 16-analyst single-source bar; on the doc's own ~¥825 bn QUICK estimate it reads B+ (+0.6 %, D). Compute next time: the band on both bars (S&P and the QUICK estimate, S&P FY2026 sat 0.8 % above IFIS); if the scenario differs between them, the OP line cannot carry the call and rule 2 decides. Disproof: three of the next five sub-1 % prints move more than 4 % in the direction the OP line's sign alone would have called.
  4. **The open is not a signal on a B-band print; the 1.82 retention assumption is conditional.** Open +0.94 % (under the cell's 1 % filter), high at 09:00, low at 11:20, close −4.04 %; close/gap −4.3 (D). Both in-line-after-run-in prints (Jul-26 +1.76 → −3.59; Oct-26 +0.94 → −4.04) opened up and closed down. Compute next time: the open gap, the scenario, and whether |gap| clears 1 % before quoting the 17/18 cell. Rule: for B-band prints the open carries no weight; a gap under 1 % is "no gap". Disproof: the next two B-band prints with an up open both extend (cell 2 of 4).
  5. **A dividend surprise is a size modifier, not a direction input.** KS5's strong form fired on both legs (FY2026 final +¥210 to ¥850; FY2027 ¥900, +31 % vs ¥686.8, D); the doc said it "lifts a B− print to flat"; the print closed −4.04 %. Payout about 50 % (D: 850 × 318.2 m / 542.5 bn = 49.9 %; press 49.3 %, S). Rule: at most 0.5 pt of size and zero directional weight when any guided profit line sits under the Street; a payout step past ~45 % reads as "growth maturing" (H). Disproof: the next print pairing a dividend surprise with a sub-Street profit line closes up.
  6. **Stage 1 may not state a side before the bar is found.** Stage 1 UP 6.0 % at P(up) 0.56 had no FY2027 OP consensus and put 0.50 on scenario A; the final's DOWN 0.46 came from one number found in stage 2, the ¥832.15 bn S&P consensus (+14.0 % on the 730 plan), plus the OP = BP convention; P(A) 0.50 → 0.13, P(C) 0.15 → 0.29; Brier 0.3136 → 0.2116 (D). Stage 1's scenario B described the outcome's shape exactly but at 0.35 weight. Compute next time: when the bar for the trigger metric is missing at stage 1, write P(up) as a range, not a point, and record which number would close it. Disproof: across the next five calls, a stage-1 point P(up) made without the bar beats the final on Brier 3 of 5 times.
  7. **Size headline when the call is a coin.** 5.5 vs 4.04: the mixture E|r| 4.85 would have been 0.81 off, the called-side conditional −4.5 0.46 off, the modal B− down size −4 0.04 off (D); the blend toward the unconditional October median 6.1 cost 0.65 pp. Rule: when P(up) is within 0.10 of 0.50, the called-side conditional is the headline size and E|r| the second line. Disproof: E|r| beats the called-side conditional on 3 of the next 5 such calls.
  8. **Every stage-1 "what misses" item gets a switch row or one line saying why it was dropped.** Stage 1 named "Japan Q4 margin down, yen/tariff cost language that caps the guide"; the page (M) guided Japan BP "steady", GPM "to contract slightly", forward-rate cost impact "significant"; GU BP 31.5 came in 15 % under the doc's ~37 (D). None had a row. Disproof: none needed; a process rule.
  9. **Tokyo data rules (M).** Pull the close from two settled-type sources, the N225 close from the Nikkei daily CSV, and the ~15:37 Nikkei article for the QUICK bar, in that order, at the first grading wake; re-read FACTS to its last line before stamping a resolution (the first draft said "single-source" and "N225 not retrieved" after FACTS had been updated at 07:14 UTC). A press intraday low (−4.48 %, S) that sits outside the settled row's range (low −4.25 %, M) is not used.

  - Pre-release research calls: Foxconn HIT, RPM HIT, Samsung HIT, Quanta MISS, LEVI HIT, APLD provisional HIT, PEP MISS, Fast Retailing provisional HIT = 6 of 8 (5 of 8 if APLD's settled close is at or below 23.81). Post-release: STZ MISS. All graded calls 6 of 9 (5 of 9 on a flip).

  - Brier: this card 0.2116 vs the coin's 0.250; with the Oct 8 evening's three (0.740 vs 0.750), the last four calls 0.952 vs 1.000 (D; 0.972 on an APLD flip).
  - DOWN calls under N3 pre-release: LEVI hit, PEP miss, FR hit (provisional). No one-way lean established.

- **Measured, derived, hypothesised.**
  - Measured (M): the Oct 8 and Oct 9 stockanalysis rows and the Yahoo Japan post-close quote page (FACTS); the N225 post-close delayed quote 69,030.92 (FACTS); every FY2026 and FY2027 figure, segment line, framing phrase and dividend on the saved company page; the FY2025 base on the saved forecast page; the Street numbers and the scenario table as the call doc recorded them.
  - Derived (D): r, the gap, the low, close/gap, the volume multiple, every guide-vs-bar percentage, the implied share count and EPS, the conversion ratios, Q4 BP/OP, the excess vs the index, the z-score, the Brier scores, the size errors and the coin probabilities below, each with its arithmetic in the resolution.
  - Hypothesised (H): the NP line as the mover; the P(up|B−) 0.25 and P(up|B+) 0.50 re-mixture; the "payout past 45 % reads as maturing" claim; the RPM trigger not having fired; rules 1-7 and their seeds.
  - Secondary (S): FY2026 OP 743.1 and FY2027 OP 830.0 (press copies of the tanshin); the intraday index figures; the "second-largest drag" reading; the press reasons for the fall; the payout 49.3 %.

- **Alternative interpretation (H).** First: on the intraday figures alone — the index opened −0.77 %, was −0.5 % mid-session, and Fast Retailing was the second-largest drag in the afternoon (S) — a −4 % close reads as a market day as much as the call's mechanism. What the data does to that reading: the post-close quote has the index at −0.02 % (M); FR's own 8.54 % weight accounts for about −0.35 pp of the index (D), so the rest of the index was up about +0.33 pp, and the official close would have to come in near −4 % for FR's excess to reach zero. The market-day reading survives only if the Nikkei CSV row differs from the quote by that much; it is held open until the row is read. Second: at n = 9 with every call near 0.50, the tally still carries no information. A fair coin lands 6 or more of 9 about 25 % of the time (D: (84 + 36 + 9 + 1) / 512 = 130 / 512) and 7 or more of 9 about 9 % of the time (D: 46 / 512); the research's 6 of 9 is an ordinary coin outcome and the coin benchmark's own 7 of 9 is the less likely one. Third: this direction hit may be a thin-bar accident. On the doc's ~¥825 bn QUICK estimate the OP line read above the Street and pointed UP; the side held because the mixture carried a 0.35 conditional on the in-line band and because a line the model did not carry (NP −3.56 % vs the Street, D) was what the press named. What carries information is not the hit rate: it is that the pre-named legs did what the table said (KS1/KS2 not fired, KS4/KS5 fired), the guide centre sat 0.2 sd from the print, and the rules above each have a disproof.

- **Official closes, 2026-10-09 11:36 UTC (M, Nasdaq historical rows for 10/08).** LEVI 19.05 (−2.36 %, HIT), APLD 23.85 (+0.17 %, HIT — the provisional flag is lifted, no flip), PEP 128.34 (+3.73 %, MISS). All three match the two settled-type sources read at 00:42 UTC to the cent; the rows posted roughly 15 hours after the close. Official opens: LEVI 19.00, APLD 24.35, PEP 124.305 — the 1-minute chart's 9:30 points (18.88 / 24.07 / 124.605) were not the opening prints, so the day-of gap-retention figures above are superseded (APLD 0.07, PEP 8.1, LEVI 0.90 on the official opens). Rule 8 gains: the opening print, like the close, comes from the settled row, not the first chart point. Tallies stand as written in the Fast Retailing entry: pre-release research 6 of 8, all graded 6 of 9, coin 7 of 9.

- **Official close, Fast Retailing, 2026-10-09 12:01 UTC (M).** stockanalysis and the Yahoo Japan daily table agree on every field: Oct 9 close 71,780, Oct 8 close 74,800, r −4.04 %, HIT. Provisional flag lifted; tallies stand (pre-release research 6 of 8, all graded 6 of 9, coin 7 of 9).

## 2026-10-12 — Quanta official row; TSMC September revenue (no call, observation only)
- **Quanta (M, TWSE STOCK_DAY):** Oct 7 close 335.00, Oct 8 close 325.50, r −2.84 %, MISS confirmed on the official row (the Oct 8 grade used the stockanalysis row, which matched to the tick). Oct 12 close 322.00, a further −1.08 %: the miss did not mean-revert over the next session.
- **TSMC (no call was made; nothing is scored).** The ad hoc call scheduled for Oct 8 01:30 UTC never happened (the wake was read after the release), so there is no prediction to grade and none is back-dated. Observation for the monthly-revenue class (M, Yahoo TW revenues API and TWSE rows): September 2026 revenue NT$511.86 bn, −0.57 % MoM, +54.65 % YoY; Q3 +9.47 % QoQ by the monthly sum. Released Thu Oct 8 at the 13:30 Taipei close as scheduled. Grading window as it would have been: close(Mon Oct 12) / close(Thu Oct 8) − 1 = 2,540 / 2,550 − 1 = **−0.39 %** (D); the TSM ADR traded Thu-Fri in New York in between. The coin benchmark for that string (first hex digit a → DOWN) would have scored a hit on a −0.39 % move; without a research call that is a coin result, not evidence. Oct 8 itself: 2,585 → 2,550 (−1.35 %) before the print, on the day the sector sold off (hynix −2.8 %, Samsung −1.3 %).
- **Process lesson, repeated from Oct 8 and now with a cost:** a scheduled pre-release call that depends on a live wake can be lost to an idle session. Rule: make and lock the call at the previous wake (the day-before refresh), and let the day-of wake only refresh it. Disproof: none needed; a process rule. Status: not yet applied — the Oct 13-16 slate calls will be the first test.

Written 2026-10-13 22:20 UTC. Every number here is from one of the seven grading results or from this file; arithmetic on them is marked D.

- **Running tallies (M, running record; tonight's seven provisional). Recomputed from the Oct 8 and Oct 9 entries, not from the task relay.**
  - Prior record in this file: pre-release research Foxconn, RPM, Samsung, LEVI, APLD, FR hits, Quanta and PEP misses = 6 of 8; post-release STZ miss; all graded 6 of 9. Coin 7 of 9. Brier for the last four scored calls 0.230 + 0.240 + 0.270 + 0.2116 = 0.9516 vs 1.000 (D).
  - **Bank cluster counted as one entry** (pre-registered per the task relay; the pre-registration is not in this file, so the convention is stated here). Convention used: the cluster's side is the sign of its mean P(up), (0.49 + 0.47 + 0.53 + 0.50) / 4 = 0.4975 → DOWN by 0.0025 (D), which is also the sign of its mean signed size, (−2.5 − 1.3 + 2.0 + 1.5) / 4 = −0.075 (D); its outcome is the mean r, (−3.08 − 0.82 − 0.23 − 5.60) / 4 = **−2.43 %** (D) → one **HIT**. The individual-hit majority is 2-2 (WFC, JPM hit; GS, C miss) and cannot decide; under a strict majority rule the entry is a tie scored 0.5. A hit decided by a 0.0025 probability margin is itself a coin outcome and is flagged as such.

Running since Oct 8: 11 calls 2.5619 vs 2.750 (mean 0.2329 vs 0.250); cluster as one, 8 entries 1.8180 vs 2.000.
  - Direction record under N3 pre-release: DOWN calls LEVI hit, PEP miss, FR hit, UNH, DPZ, WFC, JPM, JNJ hits = 7 of 8; UP calls APLD hit, GS miss, C miss = 1 of 3. Tonight's lean was 5 DOWN / 2 UP on a tape where 7 of 7 fell.

- **Lessons as hypotheses, marked (D from the seven resolutions unless noted).**
  1. **Lesson 7 sizing vs the scoreboard shrink.** Every call was within 0.10 of 0.50, so FR rule 7 (called-side conditional as the headline) applied to all seven; the shrunk sizes were used instead. Per name, the shrink **helped** on the three prints with |r| < 1 (UNH 3.0 → 2.77 vs the 4.9 conditional's 4.67; JPM 1.3 → 0.48 vs 1.7's 0.88; GS 2.0 → 1.77 vs 2.7's 2.47) and **hurt** on the four with |r| ≥ 3 (DPZ 2.0 → 2.76 vs 2.9's 1.86; WFC 2.5 → 0.58 vs 3.3's 0.22; JNJ 1.2 → 4.53 vs 1.6's 4.13; C 1.5 → 4.10 vs 2.8's 2.80). By count the conditional won 4 of 7; by points the two tie, 16.99 vs 17.03, because the shrink's three wins (1.90 + 0.40 + 0.70 = 3.00) equal its four losses (0.90 + 0.36 + 0.40 + 1.30 = 2.96); the options sizes tie with both at 17.01 and no-change loses at 20.45 (D). The size error is set by which mode prints, not by the shrink factor: the sizes that would have scored best were the own-mixture optimum or no-change on the small three (UNH 1.3 → 1.07; GS signed optimum +0.5 → 0.27; JPM down-leg median 1.21 → 0.39) and the options or the modal down leg on the large four (DPZ 5.3 → 0.54; JNJ raw straddle 3.58 → 2.15; C B-size 4.0 → 1.60; WFC 3.3 → 0.22). FR rule 7's own test (E|r| vs the conditional) on the three names with both recorded: WFC tie (0.21 vs 0.22), DPZ conditional (1.86 vs 2.01), UNH E|r| (2.7 → 2.47 vs 4.67): 1 / 1 / 1. Companion record "conditional vs chosen" now PEP, FR, DPZ, WFC, JNJ, C for the conditional, UNH, JPM, GS against = 6 of 9.
  2. **UNH large-gap fade (Foxconn gap-retention hypothesis).** Pre-market +2.8 % at 05:52 ET on the wire, +5.01 % at 09:29, open +4.4 to +5.1 %, close −0.23 %: retention −0.05 against the cell's ~0.75 (n = 4), the first sign reversal in 13 UNH prints, positive gaps ≥ 4 % now 5/5 faded (UNH grade, M/D). **Helped** on the direction of the fade, **hurt** on its size. Pooled tonight: up-gaps JPM +2.12 / +1.70 → −0.82 (retention −0.39 / −0.51, fourth reversal in 13) and GS +3.47 → −0.23 (retention −0.07) also reversed — up-gaps 0 of 3 held; down-gaps JNJ −3.50 → −5.73 (1.64, extended) and DPZ −7.68 → −4.76 (0.62, partly retained); C −0.44 % = no gap. FR rule 4 ("the open is not a signal") helped on UNH, JPM, GS and C, beyond the B-band it was written for. Mechanism recorded in the charts (M): the call was bought, the regular session gave the gap back (UNH −6.3 % 09:31-10:11; GS −3.8 % to the 10:05 low) and the sign was made 15:45-16:00 (GS −0.32, JPM −0.80, WFC ~−0.5, C −0.33 relative).
  3. **Eve-of-print signals (Oct 8 rule 7: size input, not direction).** UNH −2.12 % eve lag → −0.23 % and −0.67 vs the MCO basket: the rule's helped clause (A print, |r| < 3) — **helped**. WFC: the relay names eve relative selling; the WFC grading result carries no eve figure, so none is quoted; the print closed −3.08 % and −1.47 vs EW8, direction-consistent, rule-7 gate (≤ −3 % on ≥ 2× ADV) unverified — **neutral, logged**. C eve +1.76 % / +1.37 relative → −5.60 %: the "eve buying as direction" cell goes 4/4 → 4/5 and the document's own alternative (the eve pre-priced the Sep 14 line) was right — **hurt** for the direction reading. JNJ run-in (+4 % five-day) → sold, cell 1/5 up — **helped**. Net: eve selling was followed by down twice, eve buying by down twice; on a 7-of-7 down tape the eve sign predicted nothing beyond the tape's. Rule 7's "informed" disproof count (3 of 5 close up): LEVI down, PEP up, UNH down (gate not met), WFC down (gate unverified) — 1 of 4 up, no gate-clean member added.
  4. **De-rated-base rule (STZ H2, 2 of 2 going in) after GS / JPM / C.** GS: the second-largest beat in its table from the deepest drawdown in the sample closed −0.23 %, own lowered-bar cell 3/3 → 3/4, cross-name de-rated-base 4/6; the forward line (backlog "decreased") was carried as a ±1 size input and under the post-lock gates the print was B′ — the LEVI template (A-print from a sold-off base with a forward negative, sold) is 2/2 — **hurt as applied**. C: the base was not de-rated on the relative test (d21 −5.0 vs EW8 −9.6, d63 −1.0 vs −10.4, 1.31× TBV vs 0.93× a year earlier; C grade, D) and the negative-drift cell was cut from crushed bases — **hurt by misapplication**, cell 2/3. JPM: d21 −6.2 % but no beat, the raise was bought intraday (+3.0 % high) and not held — **untested**. JNJ's inverse (no fear priced → P(up|beat) ≤ 0.50) — **helped**, beat-and-raise cell 4/9. DPZ: the de-rated base bought back +3.17 % of a −7.68 % open — a size cushion only. Revision carried into rule 3 below: the conditional needs a relative de-rating, a beat and a forward positive; with a forward negative it inverts.
  5. **Held-midpoint configuration (JNJ, UNH).** Neither printed a held midpoint: UNH replaced its range with a floor ("at least $20.50", +0.65 vs 19.85) and JNJ nudged +0.06 to 11.10 (0.03 over the quarterly-basis street, 0.08 under the vendor FY). Both were sold (−0.23, −5.73). JNJ hypothesis 1 **helped** on direction with the mechanism rated minor by the press (talc, MedTech, margins first); UNH **neutral** (configuration absent, same outcome). The shape both shared is RAISE-FLOW: raise − beat = +0.08 (UNH) and 0.00 (JNJ), with the implied next quarter 2.20 vs 2.13 (+3.3 %, UNH) and 2.91 vs 2.94 (−1.0 %, JNJ). Oct 8 rule 2 (next-quarter implied below consensus → P(up|raise) ≤ 0.30): JNJ **helped**, cell 0/4 up; UNH is outside the rule's claim (above consensus, still sold) — **neutral**, and a mark against reading the rule as two-sided.
  6. **Compressed single pass vs the full workflow.** Compressed (JPM, GS, JNJ, C): 2 hits of 4, Brier 0.9282 (mean 0.2321). Full (UNH, DPZ, WFC): 3 of 3, 0.6821 (mean 0.2274). Separation 0.005 per call on Brier — **neutral**; on hits 3/3 vs 2/4, n too small. What separates is the error type: the compressed misses were specification errors visible from data on disk before the lock (C's KS2 keyed on the Sep 14 remark; GS's backlog sentence as a size input; JPM's Corporate run-down and buyback with no row; JNJ's segment consensus never pulled), the full-workflow errors were size (DPZ 2.76, UNH 2.77). The compressed calls' own post-lock checks would have scored 0.2116 + 0.2601 + 0.2304 + 0.2116 = 0.9137 vs 0.9282 locked (−0.0145, D): C −0.0384 and GS −0.0208 from factual corrections, JPM −0.0093, JNJ +0.054 from re-weighting toward the coin.
  7. **Post-lock skeptics vs the lock.** JNJ: skeptics 0.51 UP (Brier 0.2601) vs the lock 0.42 (0.1764) — the lock was right. C: skeptics 0.45 / 0.47 DOWN (0.2025 / 0.2209) vs 0.50 UP (0.2500) — the skeptics were right. JPM: skeptic 1 0.52 UP (0.2704) vs 0.47 (0.2209) — lock right. GS: skeptic 1 0.47 DOWN (0.2209) vs 0.53 (0.2809) — skeptic right. 2-2 on the side; summed Brier of the skeptic sides 0.9631 vs the locks' 0.9282 (D). Every skeptic move went against the lock's side; they were right exactly when the lock's premise was factually wrong (C: a pre-released line mapped to A; GS: the forward negative ungated) and wrong when they re-weighted without a new fact (JNJ R1-R4; JPM's raise weight). What that says: the skeptic stage is a premise audit, not a side-picker; its side belongs on the sensitivity line (FR's one-way-lean treatment) unless it names a factual error — rule 6 below.
  8. **Unverified secondary remark (C).** KS2 fired on "we expect to exceed this year's return target" (M), a line the Sep 14 conference remark "a bit above 11 %" (S) had already given; the document held the remark and never computed what it implied: Q4 NI to common $2.69-3.54 B at FY 11.0-11.5 % = Q4 EPS $1.63-2.14 vs the 2.39 consensus (C grade, D). A switch whose condition a secondary-sourced pre-print remark already met carried no information and the mapping KS2 → A was worth P(up) 0.503 → 0.434. **Hurt**; FR rule 8 ("every stage-1 what-misses item gets a row") hurt here and on JPM (buyback, no row), GS (call-day charge, no row) and JNJ (talc accrual inside an unscorable KS5): 0 helped / 4 hurt.
  9. **Day-of lock process miss (Oct 12 process rule, first test).** The day-before lock was met for UNH, DPZ and WFC and not for the four compressed calls (C post-lock 5; JNJ H-E). The day-of refresh was written for none: WFC's flip table had "JPM ≤ −2 % before 7:00 → size −3.0" and JPM printed −2.0 to −3.9 % on all 29 prints 06:31-06:59 ET (M) — unwritten, cost 0.50 pt (0.58 vs 0.08); C's own 40-minute check reached DOWN 0.46 and the lock was not reopened — cost the side (0.2500 vs 0.2116), the Quanta lesson repeated; GS's and JPM's checks moved toward the outcome and had no mechanism to change the line. No governing-rule sentence was written on any of the four (GS MS14, C post-lock 5). **Hurt**, with a measured cost on WFC and C.
  10. **Ex-item handling (Oct 8 rule 1) and the vendor split (JNJ).** JNJ: the KS1a/KS1b design (company-basis beat vs a wire "miss" label) held — 2.59 vs 2.53 fired, no wire called a miss, and the label shock came from GAAP $0.09 instead, a line with no switch — **neutral** on the split, hurt on the missing GAAP row. WFC: KS1 scored ex the $0.056 discrete tax item (1.81 ≤ 1.82) put the print in D-light while the Street scored a beat; stock −3.08 — **helped**. JPM: item $0.18 < 25 % of the bar, scored on 5.75 → miss — **helped**. UNH: $610 M favorable development = $0.55/share = 97 % of the beat was not treated as an item (EPS_ex 4.14 = in line) — **hurt by omission**; extension: reserve development and other non-recurring gains inside adjusted EPS count as items for the ≥ 25 % test. Pooled 2 helped / 1 hurt / 1 neutral.
  11. **Other prior rules, pooled.** Oct 8 rule 5 (cut-with-beat cell seeded at P(up) 0.50 from a de-rated base): DPZ printed cut-with-EPS-beat from a de-rated base and closed −4.76 — **hurt**, cell PEP +3.73 / DPZ −4.76 = 1/2. Oct 8 rule 6 (every conditional in the flip table; floor/cap 0.20/0.80): the caps did not bite and the UP-pointing conditionals were the error — see rule 3 below. Oct 8 rule 4 (relative line): applied to the banks and UNH — **helped as a diagnostic** (GS idio +1.97 vs the called +2.0; JPM +1.08; C −4.30; WFC −1.47). FR rule 1 (guide centre, |z| ≤ 1): UNH FY z +2.9 as a floor — one |z| > 1 against. FR rule 2 (lines below the headline need switches): WFC capital leg helped (substance fired at r −3.08); JNJ GAAP, JPM Corporate, C implied Q4 had none — 1 helped / 3 hurt. FR rule 3 (sub-1 % is in line): WFC NII −0.9 % was headlined as a miss and the stock fell 3.08 — leaning hurt, under the rule's 4 % disproof bar; JNJ sales +0.1-0.2 % neutral. FR rule 6 (no side before the bar): JNJ's MedTech bar (LSEG 8.71 / Zacks 8.77 B, in the morning press) was never pulled — hurt. Data rules (Oct 8 rule 8, FR rule 9): applied as written and **helped** — Nasdaq quote vs the settled 10/12 row 0.27 off for UNH while CBOE matched; chart 4:00 points off the quote (GS 891.21 vs 891.91; JNJ 248.98 vs 248.63); chart headers a day stale (UNH, C); EDGAR acceptance lagged the wire on every bank and on JNJ (C 2 h 9 min; JNJ ~1 h 50 min; GS 07:32:56 vs the 07:30 tape) — KS7/KS8 must watch the tape. One-way-lean check: 5 DOWN / 2 UP flagged (JNJ H-E) and the tape fell 7 of 7 — **neutral** (the lean was rewarded; the check is for bias, and N3 now reads DOWN 7/8 vs UP 1/3).

- **Cross-name pattern (D).** The modal-scenario conditional pointed the wrong way on every UP-pointing call and the right way on nearly every DOWN-pointing one. UP-pointing (≥ 0.67): LEVI A 0.70 (−2.41), UNH A ~0.67-0.71 (−0.23), GS A 0.72 (−0.23), C A 0.70 (−5.60) = **0 of 4**. DOWN-pointing: FR B− 0.35, JNJ C 0.25, DPZ D1 0.20, WFC C/D 0.46/0.20, JPM D 0.20 right, PEP C 0.10 wrong = 5 of 6. The Oct 8 reading ("the research read the prints well and the reactions poorly") holds: the switch tables pre-named the legs that fired on all seven (UNH KS4; DPZ KS3/KS4; WFC KS5 in substance; JPM KS2/KS3; GS KS5; JNJ KS4; C KS1/KS2), and the P(up|A) priors did the damage.

- **Alternative interpretations (H).**
  1. **A down tape for both groups did the work, not the calls.** SPY −0.01 %, XLF −0.42 %, KRE −0.72 %, XLV −1.31 %, bank EW8 −1.90 to −2.00 % with the non-reporters −1.3 % (M, q files). Seven of seven closed down: an "always DOWN" line scores 7/7, the research 5/7, the coin 4/7. On the three thin calls the tape set the absolute sign against the relative line — UNH +1.08 vs XLV (−0.67 vs its MCO basket), JPM +1.08 vs EW8, GS +1.77 vs EW8 — so two DOWN hits (UNH vs sector, JPM) and the GS miss were the tape's; on the four name-specific moves (DPZ −4.59, WFC −1.47, JNJ −4.42 to −4.86, C −4.30 relative) the research was 3 of 4 on the side and 2.8-4.5 pt short on size on three. Graded on the pre-registered relative lines the count is still 5 of 7 but the members change (JPM → miss, GS → hit). The tape was itself the reporters' doing (C and WFC pulled EW8; JNJ pulled MRK −2.38 and ABBV −2.45), so on a multi-reporter day the absolute close is partly the cluster's print, and only the relative line is a clean test. What would settle it: on the next multi-reporter day with |EW basket| < 0.5 %, the UP-pointing conditionals hit.
  2. **The record is still a coin.** Individually research and coin are both 11 of 16; P(≥ 11 of 16 | fair coin) = 10.5 % (D). The cluster-as-one 10 of 13 (P 4.6 %) rests on a 0.0025 margin and on a convention the file had not recorded. The Brier edge (2.5619 vs 2.750 over 11) is what a 60-70 % hit rate at mean confidence 0.03 produces mechanically; the two misses tonight were the two least confident calls (0.50, 0.53), which is the same fact as "all seven fell".
  3. **The 0-of-4 on scenario A is the regime, not the model.** A market that sells beat-and-raise prints from de-rated bases (LEVI, UNH, GS, C; RPM and STZ the counter-cases) may be a crowded-long / de-rating phase; if it flips, the cap in rule 3 below hurts and the Oct 8 priors return. The rule is written with that disproof.

- **New lessons (each a testable rule, with the number to compute and what marks it).**
  1. **Forward-line gate (UNH, JNJ, C, WFC, DPZ; extends Oct 8 rule 2 and FR rule 2).** Before the lock, compute the implied next period from the company's guide convention and the 9M actuals: Q4_implied = new FY (floor / top / midpoint as guided) − 9M, or FY_implied = 9M + the Q4 number given; z vs the same-period consensus; for unit guides, implied FY = YTD / (1 − prior-year Q4 share). If the implied line is ≤ −1 % vs consensus, file the print in the hold/down scenario regardless of beat or raise, P(up) ≤ 0.35, size at the called-side down conditional or above. Data: JNJ 2.91 vs 2.94 (−1.0 %) → −5.73; C Q4 EPS 1.63-2.14 vs 2.39 → −5.60; WFC FY 49.93 vs 50.0 B (HOLD, not the RAISE the Q4 band read) → −3.08; DPZ stores 161 vs 170 → −4.76; UNH +3.3 % → −0.23 (the above side gave no up credit). Helped: implied ≤ −1 % and close ≤ −1 %. Hurt: implied ≤ −1 % and close ≥ +1 %. Neutral: no computable implied line. Disproof: two of the next five below-consensus prints close up ≥ +1 %, or the headline beat sign predicts r as well as the implied-line sign over the next five.
  2. **A pre-released line is a confirmation, never a raise (C; GS pre-told lines).** Before writing any guide switch, list every forward statement management has made since the last print (conference, 8-K FD, pre-release) with date and source; a switch whose fire condition that statement already meets is deleted or mapped to the hold scenario, and the implied next period is computed from the statement (rule 1). Data: C Sep 14 "a bit above 11 %" → release "exceed this year's return target" → KS2 → A at 0.70, close −5.60; GS pre-told FICC / expense / provision lines printed as pre-told and moved nothing; WFC's pre-wired NIM 2.43 printed exactly and the NII miss came from the balance leg. Helped: a re-stated line in the release and the close ≤ 0 %. Hurt: the release re-states a conference line with no new positive and the stock closes ≥ +2 %. Neutral: no pre-print forward statement existed.
  3. **Cap P(up | A) until an A print closes up (LEVI, UNH, GS, C; revises Oct 8 rule 6 and STZ H2).** Scenario A by the letter (beat + raise / beat on every operating line) has closed down 4 of 4 (−2.41, −0.23, −0.23, −5.60) while the DOWN-pointing modal conditionals went 5 of 6. Rule: P(up|A) ≤ 0.55 unless the own-name A cell shows ≥ 2 up closes in its last 4; de-rated-base credit (+0.15) only when all three hold — relative de-rating (name d21 and d63 both below the basket's, or P/TBV in the bottom half of the own 1-year range), a beat, and a forward line ≥ consensus; with a forward negative the credit inverts (LEVI template 2/2). Helped: an A print at the capped conditional closes ≤ +1 %. Hurt: an A print closes ≥ +2 %. Disproof: two of the next five A prints close up ≥ +2 %, or the next multi-reporter day with a flat basket sees the A conditionals hit (alternative 1).
  4. **Cluster-day basket term (GS, JPM, WFC, C; extends Oct 8 rule 4).** On a day with ≥ 2 same-sector reporters, pre-register β (63-day, to the EW basket) and the basket; write P(up|scenario) as P(idio > 0 | scenario) and the absolute P(up) as P(idio + β·basket > 0) with E[basket] from the cluster's own calls already on the record (JPM DOWN 1.3 and WFC DOWN 2.5 preceded the GS lock); grade sign(r) and sign(r − β·basket) as two lines, with the non-reporter basket as the sector control. Data: GS idio +1.97 vs called +2.0 with r −0.23 on EW8 −2.00 (its most negative on a GS print day); JPM +1.08 relative, −0.82 absolute; WFC −1.47 / −3.08; C −4.30 / −5.60; GS's own arithmetic puts the mixture at ~0.49-0.50 with the term in. Helped: the basket-adjusted P(up) is nearer the outcome than the unadjusted on the cluster's names (Brier). Hurt: further. Neutral: single-reporter day.
  5. **Stacked-DOWN size floor; shrink only on mixed prints (JNJ, C, DPZ, WFC vs UNH, JPM, GS).** When the side is DOWN and either ≥ 2 independent DOWN switches carry P ≥ 0.35 or rule 1's gate is on, the headline size is max(called-side down conditional, options event-only); the scoreboard shrink is allowed only when an UP leg and a DOWN leg both carry P ≥ 0.35 (the mixed prints: UNH raise + development, JPM raise + miss, GS beat + cluster). Data: the shrink lost on all four |r| ≥ 3 prints and won on all three |r| < 1; the floor would have cut JNJ 4.53 → 2.83, C 4.10 → 2.50, DPZ 2.76 → 0.54 and cost WFC 0.24 (0.58 → 0.82). Helped: a floored DOWN call lands within 1.5 pt of |r|. Hurt: |r| < 1.5 on a floored call. Disproof: the shrunk size beats the floor on 3 of the next 5 stacked-DOWN calls.
  6. **The day-of refresh is written every time, with a factual-error gate on the side (WFC, C, GS, JPM, JNJ; makes the Oct 12 rule operational).** The day-of wake writes a Day-of section with: the cluster print level at the switch's hour, the pre-market quote, the governing-rule sentence (which rule decides the side), and a verdict — "lock stands", "size changed", or "side changed". The side may change only when the check finds a factual error in a switch's premise (a pre-released line, a mis-stated gate, a wrong base); a re-weighting without a new fact goes on the sensitivity line. Data: WFC (JPM ≤ −2 % for 29 minutes → size −3.0: 0.08 vs 0.58), C (factual: the Sep 14 line → DOWN 0.46: 0.2116 vs 0.2500), GS (factual: the backlog gate → 0.51 / 1.5: 0.2601 vs 0.2809, 1.27 vs 1.77), JPM (re-weighting → 0.46: 0.2116 vs 0.2209), JNJ (re-weighting → 0.48: 0.2304 vs 0.1764 — the gate would have blocked it). Helped: a written section moves size or side toward the outcome. Hurt: away. Neutral: lock stands and no information arrived.

- **What would disprove the round's main lesson.** The main lesson is that the scenario table names the print and the forward line — the implied next period against its consensus, including lines management had already given — names the reaction, so P(up|A) is capped and the forward line gates the scenario (rules 1-3). It is disproved if, over the next five prints with a computable implied forward line, the headline beat/raise sign predicts the close at least as well as the forward-line sign; or if two of the next five scenario-A prints close up ≥ +2 % with the forward line at or below consensus; or if the next multi-reporter day with a flat group basket (|EW| < 0.5 %) sees the UP-pointing conditionals hit — in which case tonight's 0 of 4 was the tape (alternative 1) and the caps come off. A weaker reshaping, not a disproof: three of the next five UNH-type prints (forward line ≥ +3 % above consensus) closing up ≥ +2 % would make rule 1 two-sided.

- **Measured, derived, hypothesised.**
  - Measured (M): the seven Oct 13 provisional closes and the Oct 12 references (Nasdaq quote endpoint, CBOE, UNH's settled 10/12 row); the exhibit figures, guide wordings, buyback and capital figures, 8-K acceptance stamps, 1-minute chart levels, peer and ETF closes quoted in the seven grading results.
  - Derived (D): every r, Brier, size error, tally, mean, cluster convention figure, binomial probability, implied next-quarter and ex-item figure above, with the arithmetic shown here or in the grading result cited.
  - Secondary (S): the press consensus bars (LSEG, Zacks, Nasdaq), transcript quotes (C's Sep 14 remark, GS's call language, DPZ's guide wording, JNJ's call), and press attributions of the moves.
  - Hypothesised (H): the tape and read-through attributions in alternative 1; the regime reading in alternative 3; rules 1-6 and their seeds; the "error type separates the two processes" reading; the mechanism of the 15:45-16:00 sign-making.

- **Official settled rows (M, read 2026-10-14 11:42 UTC).** UNH 377.76 (r −0.20 % vs the provisional −0.23), WFC 80.74 (−3.10 vs −3.08), JPM 331.43 (−0.86 vs −0.82); DPZ 296.10, GS 891.91, JNJ 248.61, C 124.53 unchanged. No direction, coin or Brier change; the seven size errors move by at most 0.04 pt (summed called-size error 16.99 → 17.01, D). Official opens: UNH 397.625 (+5.05 %), JPM 339.97 (+1.70 %), GS 923.00 (+3.25 %), WFC 80.57, C 131.345, DPZ 287.01, JNJ 254.56. The tallies above stand.

## 2026-10-14 — ASML (Amsterdam close; provisional on one source, confirm wake Oct 15 08:00 UTC)

Written 2026-10-14 16:08 UTC. **Call DOWN 3.0 % at P(up) 0.41; realised +1.23 % → MISS; coin UP → hit; Brier 0.3481 vs 0.25; |r| error 1.77 vs the call, 2.77 vs the options ~4.0, 1.23 vs no-change** (M close from stockanalysis; D arithmetic; ASML resolution).
- **What printed:** scenario A on every gate — sales €11.66 B in line, GM 57.5 %, NI €4.96 B, Q4 guide €14.2-15.2 B (+3.5-4.6 % above the street), FY narrowed up to €44-45 B, 2027/2028 supply "will not fully meet demand", 2028 capacity beyond +30 % under investigation, €4.1 B of buybacks (M). Open +0.03 %, high +3.6 %, close +1.23 %; Europe semis −0.34 % → relative +1.57 %; the ADR in EUR +0.85 % vs the Amsterdam reference, i.e. −0.8 % on its own close.
- **Reading (D/H):** the beat-and-raise was sold relative to where the ADR already stood (fifth A-type print of the cycle: LEVI, UNH, GS, C closed down; ASML closed up only by the catch-up to its ADR). The Oct 13 rule 3 reading "A prints are sold" held in the ADR's frame and failed in the graded frame because the head start (+1.5-1.7 %) was larger than the print's own negative (−0.8 %).
- **Marks:** Oct 13 rule 3 (cap P(up|A)) **hurt on the side, helped on the size** (the A print's close +1.23 was far under the +4.5 up-size); rule 1 (forward-line gate) **neutral** (read ABOVE, which pointed up, and the side still went DOWN through the mass cut); rule 2 **neutral** (a new number was given); lesson 7 / shrink **hurt** (|r| 1.23; the MC optimum 1.8 was closest); head-start pass-through **split** (absent at the open, present by the close); quiet-tape size prior **hurt**.
- **New rule (testable).** *Positive head start plus a strong own A cell: the side is UP unless P(print ≤ −head start) > 0.5 is shown explicitly.* Compute the threshold as P(D) + P(E) + P(B) × P(r_B ≤ −c); if it is under 0.5, the call is UP at the head start's size. Helped: the next head-start print (≥ +1 %) closes on the head start's side. Hurt: it closes against it by more than the head start.
- **Process:** the one full workflow of the night produced the miss; the skeptics split 1-1 and the synthesis sided with the DOWN skeptic on the strength of the Oct 13 rules — a rule applied one print after it was written, to a name whose own cells (3 of 4 beats above the range top) argued the other way. Rule: a rule written from n ≤ 5 cross-name observations yields to an own-name cell of n ≥ 4 pointing the other way.
- **Tallies (D):** pre-release research, cluster as one: 10 of 13 (individually 11 of 17); coin: 10.5 of 14 (12 of 17). Brier running: 12 scored calls 2.9100 vs the coin's 3.000 (mean 0.2425 vs 0.250); the edge is now 0.0075 per call. DOWN calls 7 of 9, UP calls 1 of 3.

## 2026-10-14 — BAC, MS, BLK (Oct 14 close, provisional on the Nasdaq 4 pm prints; settled rows and opens at the Oct 15 11:40 UTC wake)

Written 2026-10-14 21:00 UTC. References corrected to the settled Oct 13 rows (BAC 53.96, MS 186.64, BLK 1,066.12).

| Name | Call | P(up) | r (prov.) | Hit | Coin | Brier | \|err\| vs call / options / no-change | Scenario printed | Relative line |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BAC | DOWN 2.4 | 0.32 | −4.71 % | **HIT** | UP, miss | 0.1024 | 2.31 / 1.81 (2.9) / 4.71 | D: adjusted EPS 1.08 < 1.11, trading −5 % vs the pre-released "flat", revenue −2 %, CET1 10.9 %; the known $1.2 B charge on top | −3.91 (β 0.89, EW8 −0.90) |
| MS | UP 2.5 | 0.51 | −2.70 % | **MISS** | UP, miss | 0.2401 | 0.20 / 0.70 (3.4) / 2.70 | A: EPS 3.03, revenue $20.3 B, Equities +41 %, NNA $232 B record, efficiency 67 % — sold | −1.86 (β 1.17, EW8 −0.72) |
| BLK | UP 3.0 | 0.52 | +0.80 % | **HIT** | DOWN, miss | 0.2304 | 2.20 / 3.50 (4.3) / 0.80 | A: adj EPS 15.93, inflows $205 B (LT $175 B), 11 % organic base fee growth, margin 47.8 % — bought, dragged by the basket | +2.61 (β 0.86, basket −2.11) |

- **Bank cluster as one (BAC + MS):** mean P(up) 0.415 → DOWN; mean r −3.70 % → **HIT**; the cluster coin (UP, UP) → miss. BLK separate: HIT. Day: cluster-as-one 2 of 2 on the US prints, 2 of 3 with ASML; individually 2 of 4.
- **Tallies (D):** pre-release research, cluster as one **12 of 15** (individually 13 of 20); coin 10.5 of 16 (individually 12 of 20). Brier running: 15 scored calls **3.4829 vs the coin's 3.750** (mean 0.2322 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 8 of 10; UP calls 2 of 5.
- **Oct 13 rules marked by these outcomes.** Rule 1 (forward-line gate): no computable gate on any of the three → **neutral**. Rule 2 (pre-released lines are confirmations): BAC *missed* its own pre-released trading line and fell 4.7 % → the rule's converse is the live form: a pre-released line that is missed is information — **helped**, and the leg "below the pre-released line" now gets its own probability. Rule 3 (cap P(up|A) ≤ 0.55 unless the own A cell is ≥ 2 of 4 up): the exemption was applied to MS (4/4 cell; A print sold, −2.70) and BLK (4/4 cell; A print bought, +0.80, +2.6 relative) and to ASML (DOWN with the cap; A print +1.23) — A prints this cycle 2 of 7 up absolute (LEVI, UNH, GS, C, MS down; ASML, BLK up): **the exemption is removed**; P(up|A) ≤ 0.55 for every name until two A prints close ≥ +2 % in the running record. Rule 4 (cluster basket term): **helped as a diagnostic** on all three (BAC and MS fell on their own; BLK was bought and dragged). Rule 5 (stacked-DOWN size floor): BAC floor 2.9 vs anchor 2.4 vs |r| 4.71 → **helped** (1 of 1). Rule 6 (mandatory day-of refresh): written for all four; nothing readable before the US cut-off because the pre-market opens after it → **neutral**, and the flip tables for pre-open US prints must key on observables available before the cut-off (overnight filings, Asia, index futures, the first reporter's pre-market move).
- **New lessons (testable).** 1. *Beat magnitude vs the own median beat.* MS beat by +7 % against a 11-35 % own record and was sold; add to every KS8-type row a leg "beat below half the own median beat = soft print (B, not A)". Helped: the next such print closes down; hurt: it closes up ≥ +2 %. 2. *Basket term in the headline size.* BLK: idio +2.6 vs absolute +0.8 on a basket −2.1 with a live driver; size = idio-size + β × E[basket], E[basket] from the prior day's move × its own one-day autocorrelation (compute, do not assume zero). Helped: the next print with a basket move ≥ 1 % lands within 1 pt of the adjusted size. 3. *First reporter's pre-market move is the cluster switch.* BAC −4.7 % at 06:45 ET preceded MS's 07:30 print and MS's −2.7 %; key the same-morning switch on it. 4. *Reference-close data rule (third instance, now a rule):* the Nasdaq quote endpoint's 4 pm field is a last-sale print (BAC 53.94 vs 53.96, MS 186.66 vs 186.64, BLK 1,066.86 vs 1,066.12, UNH Oct 12); the reference is stockanalysis/CBOE until the settled row posts.
- **Alternative interpretation (H).** The three closes were one tape: SPY −1.07 %, the alt managers −2.1 %, the banks −0.7 to −0.9 %; BAC and MS fell with their own news on top of it and BLK's +0.8 is a strong print netted against the tape. On that reading the hit on BAC is the tape's as much as the print's (BAC −3.9 relative says mostly the print), and the MS miss is a regime fact (beats sold), not a model fact. What would disprove the main lesson (A prints sold): two of the next five A-type prints closing up ≥ +2 % absolute with a flat basket.
- **Measured / derived / hypothesised.** M: the 8-K acceptance times and exhibit figures, the Oct 14 4 pm prints and after-hours quotes, the settled Oct 13 rows, the peer closes. D: every r, Brier, error, basket, relative line, tally. S: the street bars. H: the mechanism of MS's sale (FICC −7 %, the small beat, BAC's lead), the autocorrelation sizing rule before it is computed.
- **ASML confirmation (M, 2026-10-15 08:02 UTC):** stockanalysis row unchanged on re-read (+1.23 %); Yahoo has no Oct 14 row, so the grade stands on one source. Day two: ASML −5.7 % intraday at 08:00 UTC Oct 15 — the beat was sold on the day after the print, outside the grade window; the one-day window measured the catch-up, the two-day window would have measured the sale.

The shrink line 1.8 would have been closer (0.2): the fourth print in a row where the smaller size won on a sold print.

## 2026-10-15 — Standing lessons after the Oct 13-15 round (consolidated; each is a hypothesis with its current record)

1. **Cost does not buy accuracy.** Oct 14's four full-agent calls (about 2 M agent tokens each) went 2 of 4; the single-pass TSMC call (four data pulls) was the closest of four models. Record: compressed 3 of 5 hits, full workflow 4 of 7 — no separation. Rule: one research pass, no agent fan-out, unless a specific unknown needs it.
2. **Predict the crowd's digestion, not the data.** The user's framing, now with evidence: the print was read right on 10 of 12 scenario tables this week and the close still went the other way on 5 of them. Every call carries a "how the crowd digests it" paragraph written as my own view (macro, positioning, narrative), separate from the arithmetic; the grading records which layer won.
3. **Sold-print regime (live).** Beat-and-raise prints from sold-off bases closed down 6 of 8 this cycle (LEVI, UNH, GS, C, MS, TSMC; up: ASML on its ADR catch-up, BLK). Disproof: two of the next five such prints close up ≥ +2 % with a flat basket.
4. **Size on sold prints: the smaller number wins.** The shrink line beat the called-side conditional on 4 of 4 sold prints this week (ASML, MS, BLK, TSMC) and lost on the big down moves (JNJ, C, BAC). Rule under test: size to the shrink line when the side is UP against the regime; size to the conditional or the options benchmark when the side is DOWN with the regime.
5. **Day one is not the digestion.** Opened-up-closed-down on 6 of 8 US prints; ASML sold on day two (−5.7 %). The scoreboard grades one close, so the call must be about that close, but the record keeps the day-two line to learn the lag.
6. **Release clock before every lock (M from the company calendar).** Missed twice (BLK 07:30 ET call, MS 09:30 ET call, TSMC home-session publication). A home-session release makes the home close the grade.
7. **Reference-close data rule.** Nasdaq's quote-endpoint 4 pm field is a last-sale print; stockanalysis/CBOE match the settled row (4 of 4 instances). Use them first.
8. **Pre-released lines cut both ways.** Met = no information (C, GS); missed = information (BAC −4.7 % on trading below its own "flat" line). Give the "below the pre-released line" leg its own probability.
9. **Own-name cells and cross-name rules.** When they agree (TSMC) the call is easy; when they disagree (ASML: own cell up, rule down; MS: own cell up, regime down) the regime won 2 of 2 this week — the own-cell exemption is withdrawn until the regime breaks.
10. **Process.** Lock the evening before; a day-of refresh for US pre-open prints cannot read the pre-market before the cut-off, so its flip tables must key on overnight filings, Asia and the first reporter's move; grade with a script and a short note, never a grading workflow (the Oct 13 grading run alone cost 2.1 M agent tokens).

Running record (D): pre-release research 13 of 16 with the bank cluster counted as one (14 of 21 individually); coin 11.5 of 17; Brier 0.227 per call vs 0.250. DOWN calls 9 of 11, UP calls 2 of 5 — the lean is the regime, not a bias to be corrected until the regime breaks.
- **Oct 14 official settled rows (M, read 2026-10-15 11:41 UTC):** BAC 51.39 (r −4.76 % vs the provisional −4.71), MS 181.51 (−2.75 vs −2.70), BLK 1,073.79 (+0.72 vs +0.80); no direction, coin or Brier change; size errors 2.36 / 0.25 / 2.28. Official opens: BAC 49.83 (−7.65 %: the after-hours anchor 52.7 was 5.4 pt too high — hypothesis 1 **hurt**; the known one-off was a floor on the fear, not the anchor), MS 180.45 (−3.32 % on an A print: the sale was decided before the open, KS6's open leg not fired, hypothesis 2 **hurt**), BLK 1,088.63 (+2.11 %, faded to +0.72: positive-gap retention cell 5/5 → 5/6). Opened-up-closed-lower now 7 of 9 US prints this week (BLK joins); the two straight-down names (BAC, MS) opened on the low side of their close.
- **TSMC release content and day two (M, 2026-10-16 06:00 UTC):** GM 66.4 %, OPM 58.7 %, Q4 guide +14 % q/q, FY2026 raised to "around 44 %" — an A print on every readable gate — closed −2.0 % on the day, the ADR −1.8 %, Taiwan −0.8 % on day two (provisional). Standing lesson 3 (sold-print regime) confirmed on the content: A prints 6 of 8 down stands with TSMC now verified as A; lesson 5 (day one is not the digestion): here day two continued the sale rather than reversing it.

## 2026-10-21 — NFLX, SPCX, PM (Oct 21 closes vs Oct 20; settled stockanalysis rows, Nasdaq 4 pm prints agree; the user's screenshots match all three)

Written 2026-10-21 20:50 UTC. **NFLX: DOWN 5.0 % at P(up) 0.41, realised −5.31 % → HIT (size error 0.31), coin DOWN hit, Brier 0.1681. SPCX: DOWN 5.0 % at 0.42, realised +7.79 % → MISS (error 12.79), coin DOWN miss, Brier 0.3364. PM: DOWN 2.5 % at 0.42, realised −1.22 % → HIT (error 1.28), coin DOWN hit, Brier 0.1764.** Cost: one grading pass (two data pulls, three exhibit reads), no agents.

**The lessons first.**
1. *SpaceX — the second print of a new issuer is not a repeat of the first.* I built the call on the Aug-4 template (double beat sold −13.6 % on capex) and on the sold-print regime, and gave the "segment inflection" case 0.30 even after the post-lock exhibit read showed Cursor consolidating in Q3 and $14.1 B of cloud agreements. The print was revenue +87 % q/q with capex +7 % q/q and a swing to a $4.4 B GAAP profit; the crowd bought it +7.8 % on a +8.6 % gap. Rule under test: the capex-sold mechanism needs capex growing faster than revenue; when a consolidation or a new segment adds revenue at scale, the first print's fear is answered on the revenue line and the A scenario deserves ≥ 0.45, not 0.30. Second rule: a label switch (GAAP loss vs profit) fires in both directions — a first GAAP profit on a loss-making base is a bought headline. Record: 0 of 1 for the naive repeat.
2. *Netflix — a quantified forward line cut inside an in-line print is the information.* Q3 in line, Q4 forecast inside the 10-12 % band (11.5 %), FY margin held, record buyback — and the ad-revenue line went from ~$3 B to ~$2.7 B. Sixth straight sold print, gap −6.6 % faded to −5.3 %. Standing lesson 8 (pre-released lines) extends: any line the company previously quantified (ad revenue, capex, FCF) that is cut or held below the street sets the sign even when the headline forecast is "in line". Record 3 of 3 this month (BAC trading line, PM FY line, NFLX ad line).
3. *Philip Morris — a crowded defensive absorbs a guide nuance intraday.* A record quarter (EPS 2.36 vs 2.30; oral SFP volumes turned positive, ZYN US +4.2 %) with the FY adjusted EPS held below the street (midpoint 8.365 vs 8.38, currency benefit trimmed) and a "middle or lower half" operating-income line on energy costs opened −6.4 %, traded −8.0 % and closed −1.2 %. Gap-downs extending on PM drop to 4 of 6. For pre-open prints on staples at the highs, the shrink line (1.5) was closest; the sign rule (run-up sold; guide held below the street = cut) held. The ZYN line, which I made the sign-setter, was positive and irrelevant to the open: the guide beat the KPI.
4. *Date sources.* The user's "SpaceX reports tonight" and the vendor calendar were right; the press aggregators (Nov 3) were wrong. The weekly check should rank company notice > exchange/vendor calendar > user statement > aggregators, and never void a call on aggregator doubt alone.
5. *Size: the between-choice.* NFLX 5.0 (between shrink 3.5 and conditional 7.3) was the closest of the four benchmarks; PM's shrink was closest; SPCX's size was moot. Across the last six graded calls the called-side conditional has been closest once (IBKR). Lesson 4's "conditional when DOWN with the regime" is now 3 of 5 — downgrade it to "between the shrink line and the conditional unless the name's |r| distribution is bimodal".

- **Regime status (D):** prints at or above the bar from sold-off bases: **8 of 11 down** (SPCX the third up, with ASML and BLK). SPCX is the first of the two bought A prints in five that reset the DOWN-on-good-print type to "untested"; NFLX was a B print and PM a non-de-rated base, so neither counts against it. The reset is live for the six Oct 21 after-close calls (SAP, META, IBM, LRCX, TSLA, TXN): one more bought A print among them triggers it.
- **Standing lessons marked (summary across the three):** 1 helped ×2, neutral ×1; 2 helped (NFLX, PM), **hurt (SPCX)**; 3 helped (NFLX, PM), **hurt (SPCX)**; 4 hurt (NFLX: conditional would have cost 2 pts), helped (PM: shrink), n/a (SPCX); 5 neutral (NFLX), **new form (PM: gap bought back intraday)**, held (SPCX); 6 met ×3; 7 helped (NFLX pre-lock stamp vs settled 68.69); 8 helped (NFLX, PM); 9 agreed ×3 (wrong on SPCX); 10 partly hurt (SPCX post-lock read not re-weighted).
- **Tallies (D):** pre-release research, cluster as one **17 of 21** (individually 18 of 26); coin 13.5 of 22 (15 of 26).
