The six-call streak ended with SPCX; NFLX was the closest call of the round (0.31 off).

## 2026-10-22 — SAP (Xetra close vs Oct 21; settled stockanalysis row, ADR cross-check) — first of the day's grades; the US after-close prints follow in their own wakes

Written 2026-10-22 16:12 UTC. **SAP: DOWN 3.0 % at P(up) 0.47, realised +3.21 % → MISS (size error 6.21), coin DOWN miss, Brier 0.2809.** Cost: two data pulls, one search, one page fetch.

**Excluded from the track record (2026-10-22, by the user):** the board removed SAP as a post-release call. The lock commit is stamped 17:57 UTC Oct 21 and the release came at the US close (~20:00 UTC) with a 23:00 CEST webcast, so by the timestamps the lock preceded the release; the board's decision governs. The tallies below this entry no longer include SAP; the lessons stand because they were learned.

**The lessons first.**
1. *At a coin flip, the name's own base rate breaks the tie, not the tail skew.* I had P(up) at 0.47 and chose DOWN because the down tail was fatter. The name was 10 of 12 up on prints; it printed in line with an 18 % profit beat and an upper-end steer and closed +3.2 %. Rule replacing hypothesis 6: when P(up) is within 0.05 of 0.50, side with the own-print base rate if it is ≥ 0.65 or ≤ 0.35 either way; use the skew only when the base rate is itself near 0.5.
2. *The chase-sold regime is a US-tape rule.* ASML (+1.2 %) and SAP (+3.2 %) are the two European after-US-close prints this round, both good prints from run-ups, both bought on day one: 0 of 2 for the regime. The regime's evidence is US names in the AI-capex and financials tape; for European names the own cell governs until this breaks.
3. *Day one, up-form.* Xetra opened −0.4 % on the first scan ("backlog 26 %, slightly decelerating") and the session bought it to +3.2 % on the profit line and the steer — the mirror of PM's gap-down bought back. SAP's gap-sign rule drops to 12 of 13. For European prints with a US-hours release and a next-morning open, the open is the first scan, not the digestion.
4. *Process.* The 6-K was not on EDGAR at grading; the quarterly statement text came from a wire reprint of the company's own release (treated as M for the figures). The sap.com PDF link returned an HTML stub.

- **Regime status (D):** good prints from sold-off or chased bases: **8 of 12 down** across names (SPCX, ASML, SAP, BLK the ups). SAP is a B-plus print (in line with a profit beat), so it is not the second "bought A print" that resets the DOWN-on-good-print type; the type stays "watch", regime-conditional, and US-only by rule 2. The six US after-close calls from Oct 21 were all on the called side at the open (provisional).
- **Standing lessons marked:** 1 helped; 2 hurt; 3 hurt (European name); 4 helped (shrink); 5 new form (open bought through the session); 6-7 met; 8 helped; 9 own cell won (exemption restored for European names, 2 of 4 overall); 10 met.
- **Tallies (D):** pre-release research, cluster as one **17 of 22** (individually 18 of 27); coin 13.5 of 23 (15 of 27). Brier running: 22 scored calls **4.8785 vs the coin's 5.500** (mean 0.2218 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 13 of 17; UP calls 2 of 5; post-release class (STZ, SPOT pending) separate.

## 2026-10-22 — META, IBM, LRCX, TSLA, TXN (Oct 22 close vs Oct 21), LMT and SPOT (Oct 22 close vs Oct 21; pre-open prints); settled stockanalysis rows, Nasdaq 4 pm prints agree

Written 2026-10-22 20:55 UTC. **META DOWN 6.0 → −6.98 HIT. IBM DOWN 6.0 → +0.26 MISS. LRCX DOWN 5.0 → −11.76 HIT. TSLA DOWN 4.5 → −3.08 HIT. TXN DOWN 3.5 → −1.81 HIT. LMT DOWN 3.5 → +6.96 MISS. SPOT UP 3.5 → +11.10 HIT (post-release class).** Pre-release: 4 of 6 on sign; the coin went 5 of 6. Cost: two price pulls, six exhibit reads, no agents.

**The lessons first.**
1. *The regime has split, and the split is the lesson.* Every AI-capex-linked print was sold whatever it contained: Meta (revenue above the guide top, capex low end raised) −7.0, Lam (guide above the street, supply-constrained) −11.8, Tesla (capex cut, FCF positive, the call gave nothing) −3.1, TI (Q4 guide far above the street) −1.8 in a −6 % semis day. Every non-AI good print was bought: Lockheed (every line raised, $292 B backlog, no charge) +7.0, Spotify (clean, subscribers beat) +11.1, SAP +3.2, SpaceX +7.8 yesterday, IBM (FCF guide raised) opened −4 and closed flat. **New regime statement: the AI-capex tape sells good prints; outside it, good prints from de-rated or chased bases are being bought.** The DOWN-on-good-print type resets to "untested" outside AI-capex (three bought A prints in five: SPCX, IBM, LMT) and stays "high, regime-conditional" inside it (ASML day two, TSMC, GE, Meta, Lam, TXN, Tesla).
2. *The street-above-guide rule needs the whole table.* It held on TI (sold despite a huge guide) and broke on Lockheed (bought on a 0.4 % EPS midpoint raise because the FCF guide rose 11 %, cash from operations 9 % and backlog 27 %). Rule: score the raise on every quantified line, not the EPS midpoint; a raise that lifts cash and backlog by double digits is an A print whatever the EPS line says.
3. *Chase vs flush was the wrong axis.* Lam (flushed −12.5 %) was sold −12 %; IBM (chased +4.8 %) closed flat; Lockheed (chased +10 %) was bought +7 %. The set-up into the print did not predict the sign this week; the sector (AI-capex or not) did. Hypothesis 1 on both the IBM and Lam cards: hurt.
4. *Size: the shrink rule for UP calls is the costliest rule in the ledger.* Spotify ran +11.1 % against a 3.5 % call; the own up-median (11.4) was within 0.3. Replace: on a name whose positive gaps have run (retention ≥ 1.2 on the own history), size an UP call at the gap × own retention or the own up-median, not the shrink line. The between-size (shrink-to-conditional) was closest or second on Meta, Tesla and TI; the conditional was closest on Lam and Meta; the mixture median won on Tesla and TI. The near-coin deviation toward the mixture median (Lam) cost 1.3 pt: on a bimodal name in a liquidation tape, take the conditional.
5. *Day one, intraday reversals are now the norm, not the exception.* IBM opened −4.1 % and closed +0.3 %; SAP opened −0.4 % and closed +3.2 %; PM opened −6.4 % and closed −1.2 % (yesterday); Meta spiked +8 % after hours and closed −7 %. The after-hours/opening print is the first scan; for the grade, the second scan is the session. Gap-sign rules broke on IBM (10 of 12) and SAP (12 of 13) and held with extension on Meta, Lam, Tesla, TI, LMT, SPOT.
6. *Tesla's layers.* The numbers beat on the exact line I made the main switch (capex/FCF) and the stock fell on the call. On Tesla the call sets the sign; the release sets the after-hours spike. The cash-flow switch (GE lesson) is a release-layer rule and does not transfer to a name whose crowd trades the call.
7. *Stated confidence.* I told the user 6 of 10 on Lockheed at P(up) 0.37 and lost by 10 points. The number given to a human should be the complement of P(up) on the called side, rounded down, and nothing more.
8. *Process.* The posts rule from the user is now in force: for any public take, primary sources only (the 8-K/6-K and the transcript), and the view comes from the pre-print scenario table marked against the release, never from a press paraphrase. The Meta take last night was written before the release and said so; the Tesla take was grounded on the transcript summary and the 8-K.

- **Standing lessons marked (summary across the seven):** 1 helped ×7; 2 helped (META, LRCX, TSLA, TXN, SPOT), hurt (IBM, LMT); 3 helped inside AI-capex (META, LRCX, TSLA, TXN), hurt outside (IBM, LMT); 4 helped (META, TXN, TSLA), hurt (LRCX deviation, SPOT shrink); 5 reversals and extensions as above; 6-7 met ×7; 8 helped (META capex line, IBM/LMT FCF lines in reverse), beaten by the layer rule on Tesla; 9 own cell lost to the sector on LRCX/TXN/LMT, won on SPOT; 10 met; peer-print lead 3 of 5 (GM→TSLA helped, NFLX→SPOT hurt).
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded by the board (see the SAP entry):** pre-release research, cluster as one **21 of 27** (individually 22 of 32); coin 18.5 of 28 (20 of 32). Brier running: 27 scored pre-release calls **6.0555 vs the coin's 6.750** (mean 0.2243 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 17 of 22; UP calls 2 of 5 (pre-release); post-release class 1 of 2 (STZ miss, SPOT hit). The two misses today were both bought A prints outside the AI-capex tape; the four hits were all inside it or on it (TI as a semis name).

## 2026-10-23 — BE, WDC (Oct 23 close vs Oct 22), AXP (Oct 23 close vs Oct 22, pre-open print); settled stockanalysis rows, Nasdaq 4 pm prints agree

Written 2026-10-23 20:25 UTC. **BE DOWN 5.0 → −11.35 HIT. WDC DOWN 5.0 → −3.05 HIT. AXP DOWN 2.5 → −3.68 HIT.** Three for three on sign; the coin went 2 of 3. Cost: one price pull, three exhibit reads, no agents.

**The lessons first.**
1. *Three A prints with raised guides, three sold.* Bloom raised the year for the third time and joined the S&P 500 and lost 11 %. Western Digital guided the December quarter 16 % above the street on revenue and 31 % on EPS and lost 3 %. American Express lifted its EPS guide and lost 3.7 %. The content of the print was not the trade in any of the three; the crowd's prior about the name was. Rule confirmed: classify the name first (AI-capex tape → raises are sold, now 18 of 20; own habit ≥ 7 of 8 one way → the habit governs), then read the print.
2. *The own-habit prior beats the cross-sectional regime when it is lopsided.* Thursday's lesson said non-AI good prints are being bought (Lockheed, Spotify, IBM). AmEx is non-AI and was sold, because its own record was 1 of 8 up with beats sold on the expense line and a raise that only reached the street's number (GE lesson 2, now 4 of 5). The Oct 22 coin-flip tie-break rule was the right instinct; it extends: a name-specific base rate at or beyond 7 of 8 overrides the regime statement.
3. *Bloom's after-hours print is noise.* Ten of twelve prints gapped up; this one spiked after hours (the X post asked why it was red by morning) and opened −6.8 %. The gap-fade habit now includes gaps that reverse before the open. For Bloom, grade the open, not the 16:05 tape.
4. *Size by tape, again.* The options benchmark was closest on Bloom and AmEx; the shrink/mixture median on Western Digital. The between-size (the Oct 21 rule) produced errors of 1.2 and 2.0 on the two orderly prints and 6.4 on the liquidation print. Rule stands with the Lam amendment: on AI-infrastructure names in a liquidation tape, take the options number.
5. *Peer-print lead* is 4 of 6 (COF → AXP helped). *Same-evening pairs* (BE/WDC) moved together, both sold: the pair test says the sector, not the base, set the sign — consistent with Thursday.

- **Standing lessons marked (summary):** 1 helped ×3; 2 helped ×3 (the digestion was the multiple, the peak, and the expense line respectively); 3 helped ×3 (two inside the AI tape, one by the own-habit route); 4 helped (WDC, AXP), hurt (BE); 5 extended ×3; 6-7 met; 8 helped in reverse ×3 (raised lines sold); 9 own cell won (AXP), lost to the regime (BE), agreed (WDC); 10 met.

## 2026-10-26 — VZ (Oct 26 close vs Oct 23; pre-open print; settled stockanalysis row, Nasdaq 4 pm print agrees)

Written 2026-10-26 21:40 UTC. **VZ: UP 3.5 % at P(up) 0.58, realised −3.49 % → MISS (size error 6.99), coin DOWN hit, Brier 0.3364.** Cost: one price pull, one exhibit read, no agents.

**The lessons first.**
1. *A dated sector overhang beats the name's own habit.* Verizon printed its best third quarter in five years (406k net adds, a third guide raise, FCF +12 %, a $2 B AWS deal) and fell 3.5 % while AT&T and T-Mobile were flat. The SpaceX fourth-carrier discount was not bought back by one quarter of numbers, as my own alternative interpretation said it might not be. The own-habit rule (≥ 7 of 8) is now 1 of 2: it worked on AXP, where the habit pointed with the tape, and failed here, where it pointed against a live sector overhang. Amendment: the own-habit prior governs only when no dated sector overhang is in force; with one, the overhang takes the side and the habit takes the size.
2. *The unquantified line is the one that gets sold.* Every line Verizon quantifies in its guide was raised; the stock fell on consolidated revenue (equipment −9 %), which the guide does not address and the street models at ~$34.85 B. Standing lesson 8 extends: on a name whose guide covers service revenue but not total revenue, the total-revenue consensus is the hidden line, and a miss there outranks a raise on the quantified ones.
3. *Relative strength into a sector shock is positioning, not immunity.* Verizon fell least on Friday's SpaceX news two sessions before its print; I read that as the crowd separating it. It was the crowd holding it for the print, and the print gave them the exit. Record for "least-hit name printing first": 0 of 1.
4. *UP calls.* The pre-release UP record is now 2 of 6. The size rule (up-median, no shrink) was not tested because the side was wrong; it stays, but the side rule for UP calls tightens: UP requires the own habit AND no dated overhang AND a non-AI tape, or the head-start exemption.

- **Standing lessons marked:** 1 helped; 2 hurt; 3 n/a; 4 n/a; 5 gap extended; 6-7 met; 8 hurt in reverse; 9 own cell lost to the sector; 10 met.
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded:** pre-release research, cluster as one **24 of 31** (individually 25 of 36); coin 21.5 of 32 (23 of 36). Brier running: 31 scored pre-release calls **6.8484 vs the coin's 7.750** (mean 0.2209 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 20 of 25; UP calls 2 of 6; post-release class 1 of 2.

## 2026-10-27 — HSBC (London close vs Oct 26) and NOVN (SIX close vs Oct 26); pre-open home-exchange prints; settled stockanalysis rows, ADR rows agree on sign

Written 2026-10-27 17:20 UTC. **HSBC: DOWN 2.5 % at P(up) 0.46, realised −5.42 % → HIT (size error 2.92), coin DOWN hit, Brier 0.2116. NOVN: DOWN 2.0 % at P(up) 0.43, realised +1.02 % → MISS (size error 3.02), coin DOWN miss, Brier 0.3249.** Cost: two price pulls, two 6-K exhibits, one web search (returned nothing current; the attribution below is my own reading of the releases and the peer rows).

**The lessons first.**
1. *A pre-announced cost step-up is a lowered bar, not a nuance.* Novartis told the market in July that H2 costs would rise; I filed that as the thing the defensive crowd would sell on. It was the bar the crowd had already priced, and the quarter cleared it (core margin +230 bp, core EPS 2.46 vs 2.25) and raised the profit guide. Standing lesson 8 gets its third form: a pre-released *negative* line met or beaten is information on the up side. The PM analog (crowded defensive at the highs sold on a nuance) requires the company to hold or lower a line on the day; with a raise it does not apply. Record for the analog: 1 of 2.
2. *The own cell was right and I overrode it.* The European rule in this file says the own print cell governs. Novartis's cell was 7 of 12 up with all three 2026 prints bought from an opening dip; I ranked a cross-name analog above it. Same error class as standing lesson 9 in reverse: when the own cell is lopsided (≥ 7 of 12 recent, or 3 of 3 in the current year) and the cross-name rule is an analog rather than a regime, the own cell takes the side. The recent cell beat the same-quarter cell (October 2 of 2 sold): recency over seasonality, record 1 of 1.
3. *Right side, wrong mechanism counts as a half-lesson.* HSBC fell 5.4 % with UK bank peers flat to up, so the Budget-eve overhang I named did not do the work; the release did: revenue $18.6 B against a ~$19 B bar, reported PBT a touch under, the NII guide held rather than raised, CET1 at 13.9 % under the company's own 14-14.5 % range while a new $1 B buyback is announced on top of it. My table gave the $1 B buyback a 0.70 up-probability; the market priced it as expected size, no information. For banks: the buyback at the expected size is a met pre-released line (lesson 8, zero information); the swing lines are revenue against the bar and capital against the company's own stated range. Record for "buyback size sets the sign": 0 of 1.
4. *Gap-holds on European pre-open prints.* HSBC −4.7 open → −5.4 close; NOVN +1.2 open → +1.0 close. Gaps over 1 % on London prints have held sign 13 of 13; the SIX print that opened up stayed up but gave back two of its three points. Size on a European name: when the open is already past the called size, the conditional is the better line (HSBC); when the open is small, the shrink line is (NOVN). This is the same rule as the US size rule, keyed on the open, which a day-of refresh cannot see before the lock — so it stays a grading observation, not a lock rule.
5. *Near-coin calls.* Both calls sat at 0.43-0.46 and went 1 of 2, which is what 0.43-0.46 means. The stated P(up) remains honest and uninformative in this band; the board confidence of 5 of 10 on both was the right statement.

- **Standing lessons marked (HSBC / NOVN):** 1 helped / helped; 2 hurt on mechanism / hurt; 3 n/a / n/a; 4 hurt / helped on magnitude; 5 n/a / n/a; 6 met / met; 7 met / met; 8 helped in reverse (revenue, the unquantified line, sold) / hurt (lowered bar cleared); 9 own cell by skew, hit / own cell overridden, miss; 10 met / met.
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded:** pre-release research, cluster as one **25 of 33** (individually 26 of 38); coin 22.5 of 34 (24 of 38). Brier running: 33 scored pre-release calls **7.3849 vs the coin's 8.250** (mean 0.2238 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 21 of 27; UP calls 2 of 6; post-release class 1 of 2. European own-cell rule: 2 of 3 graded (ASML, HSBC hits; NOVN miss — the one where I did not follow it).
- **Pending:** PYPL, UPS, KO, GLW, BA on the Oct 27 US close (wake 21:40 UTC).

## 2026-10-27 — PYPL, UPS, KO, GLW, BA (Oct 27 closes vs Oct 26; five pre-open prints; settled stockanalysis rows, Nasdaq 4 pm prints agree)

Written 2026-10-27 21:55 UTC. **PYPL: DOWN 3.5 at 0.47, realised +2.00 → MISS (Brier 0.2809). UPS: DOWN 4.0 at 0.41, realised +5.75 → MISS (0.3481). KO: UP 2.5 at 0.50, realised −1.64 → MISS (0.2500). GLW: DOWN 6.0 at 0.33, realised −1.13 → HIT (0.1089). BA: DOWN 2.5 at 0.46, realised −10.69 → HIT (0.2116). 2 of 5; coins 3 of 5.** Cost: one price pull, five exhibits, no agents, no searches.

**The lessons first.**
1. *My own regime statement was right and I overrode it twice.* The Oct 22 statement says that outside the AI-capex tape good prints from de-rated bases are bought. PayPal and UPS are both outside the tape, both de-rated, both raised their guide tables, both were bought (+2.0, +5.75). I called DOWN on PayPal with a tail-skew tie-break (0 of 2 now, with SAP: **retired**) and on UPS with an 8-of-12 down habit that does not meet my own 7-of-8 threshold plus one July print. Rule: outside the tape, within 0.10 of the coin, the base classification decides — de-rated → UP, chased → DOWN — and the own habit overrides only at ≥ 7 of 8. The base classification itself needs a number: "chased" means a run-up to within 10 % of the 52-week high or +15 % over 21 sessions, not two green days off a low (UPS).
2. *Guided lines raised are bought even when the unguided line misses.* PayPal's revenue was light and ignored because EPS and TM$ (the lines it guides) were raised; UPS raised every line and FedEx went with it; Coca-Cola raised every line and still fell with its sector. Standing lesson 8 now has its full form: **the unguided line that misses is sold (VZ, HSBC, BA); the guided lines that are raised are bought (PYPL, UPS, NOVN); a raise of the whole table beats a miss on an unguided line, outside the AI tape; inside the tape the raise is sold whatever the table says (GLW, WDC, LRCX).**
3. *Defensives at highs trade the sector's day.* Coca-Cola printed volume +5 % and a table-wide raise and closed −1.6 % from a −2.0 gap with XLP −0.9 and PEP −1.5 on a +4 % semiconductor day. Novartis, the same shape in Zurich four hours earlier, was bought +1.0. The difference was the tape, which a lock cannot see. For crowded defensives the sign is a coin and the size is small (PM −1.2, KO −1.6, NOVN +1.0); **no UP calls on defensives at highs** (the payoff-asymmetry rule failed its first test), and DOWN calls there size at the shrink line. UP calls are now 2 of 7.
4. *The AI-capex sell is shrinking.* Bloom −11.4, WDC −3.1, Corning −1.1 (from a −4.7 low), each a print above its own guide. The regime held 19 of 21 but the fifth sell of the round was bought back to −1 on a +4 % SOXX day. Not a break (the disproof needs two bought A prints in five), but the three tests queued — hynix, Seagate, GE Vernova — carry more weight than they did this morning, and the Lam/Bloom size amendment (size to the options in a liquidation) misfired on a bounce day: in a bounce the shrink line governs the size even when the regime governs the side.
5. *A turnaround's down tail is the margin line, not the charge scan.* Boeing fell 10.7 % with no new charge: revenue light, core loss below the whole consensus range, Commercial Airplanes margin −6.5 % on mix, cash under the line. I sized for a no-news print (2.5) against an options line (4.5) that was itself under half the move. On turnaround names with miss risk on the quantified lines, size at the options line or above; the own median (2.7) was built on prints that did not miss.
6. *Near-coin calls, again.* Three calls within 0.10 of the coin went 1 of 3; the two under 0.42 (GLW, UPS) went 1 of 2. Calls at ≤ 0.42 are now 11 of 14 and calls at 0.43-0.50 are 7 of 14 — the coin. The P(up) numbers are honest about what they know. The fix is in lesson 1: the tie-break rule, not the probability.

- **Standing lessons marked (PYPL / UPS / KO / GLW / BA):** 1 helped ×5; 2 hurt / hurt / hurt / helped / half; 3 n/a / n/a / n/a / helped / n/a; 4 helped / n/a / helped / hurt / hurt badly; 5 n/a; 6 met ×5; 7 met ×5; 8 hurt / hurt / n/a / helped in reverse / helped in reverse; 9 outside-tape rule ignored / habit below threshold used / own cell lost to sector / agreed / October habit by luck; 10 met ×5.
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded:** pre-release research, cluster as one **27 of 38** (individually 28 of 43); coin 25.5 of 39 (27 of 43). Brier running: 38 scored pre-release calls **8.5844 vs the coin's 9.500** (mean 0.2259 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 23 of 31; UP calls 2 of 7; post-release class 1 of 2. Today: HSBC hit, NOVN miss, GLW hit, BA hit, PYPL/UPS/KO misses — 3 of 7 on the day.

## 2026-10-28 — SK hynix (KRX close vs Oct 27; pre-open Seoul print; Daum and stockanalysis rows agree)

Written 2026-10-28 07:30 UTC. **SK hynix: DOWN 4.0 % at P(up) 0.42, realised +10.89 % → MISS (size error 14.89, the largest of the round), coin UP hit, Brier 0.3364.** KOSPI +6.63 %, Samsung +9.01 %. Cost: three price pulls, one newsroom read, one web search (returned nothing current).

**The lessons first.**
1. *The regime's evidence does not reach past the liquidation.* All 19 sold prints behind "inside the AI tape, raises are sold" were names at or near their highs or printing on liquidation days (Oct 22-23). SK hynix printed 55 % below its high, after a 21 % index crash, with the memory tape bouncing +3 to +7 % overnight, and its own cell (10 of 12 up, three Octobers up) governed: +10.9 %, the gap extended, not faded. **Amendment to the regime statement:** inside the AI tape the regime governs at or near highs and on liquidation days; after a drawdown of 25 % or more from the high with the tape bouncing (SOXX or the sector ETF ≥ +3 % the prior session), the own cell governs the side, as it does outside the tape. Standing lesson 9 ("the regime beats the own cell") is 2 of 3 and its one loss cost more than its two wins earned. **Pre-registration for today's grades:** STX (41 % below its high, own cell 7 of 9 up) and GEV (25 % below, own cell 6 of 9) were locked DOWN under the old rule before this grade; the amended rule would have said UP on STX and coin on GEV. If both are bought today the amendment is confirmed 3 of 3; if both are sold, the hynix result was the Korean index effect and the amendment narrows to home-market bellwethers. The locked calls stand.
2. *A pre-announced line is a bar only when the company lands on it.* I scored the ₩16 T special-dividend report as a met line worth nothing (VZ, HSBC). The company announced ~₩100 T of 2026 returns. The met-line rule needs its other half: the surprise against the pre-announced number sets the sign, and a company that says "we will announce measures" without confirming the rumour's number has not pre-released anything. Record for "pre-announced return = no information": 1 of 2 (HSBC yes, hynix no).
3. *Supplier capex is not spender capex.* Inside the tape I have sold the capex line because the buyers' spending (Meta, Oracle-type) is the thing the crowd doubts. SK hynix raised its capex "significantly" and was bought, because the same release said demand exceeds supply through 2030 and LTAs cover half of 2027-28 capacity: a seller's capex raise with sold-out contracts reads as revenue. Lam (equipment, sold) is the counter-case, but Lam printed at its high. Hypothesis, 1 of 1: the capex line is sold on spenders and on suppliers at highs, bought on suppliers after a drawdown with contracted demand.
4. *Size off the live sigma, not the print history.* The own print median (2.6) and the ADR options (4.7) both sat under half the move on a name whose 63-day daily sigma was 6.4 %. When the live sigma is more than twice the historical, the print |r| should be sized at no less than one live daily sigma on the called side (6.4 here), and the up-tail of the own cell (+7.1) is the reference when the own cell is lopsided-up. This would have reduced the size error, not the direction error.
5. *Same day, opposite errors.* On Oct 27 I overrode Novartis's own cell with an analog (miss), followed Coca-Cola's payoff skew against its sector (miss), overrode PayPal's and UPS's de-rated bases with a skew and a sub-threshold habit (misses), and overrode hynix's own cell with the regime (miss). The common thread is not the regime or the own cell; it is that I reached past the evidence cell that fit the name best. Rule: before the side is set, name the single evidence cell that fits the name's configuration (base, tape, habit, pre-released lines) and state why any rule that overrides it has evidence from the same configuration. If it does not, the cell governs.

- **Standing lessons marked:** 1 helped; 2 hurt; 3 hurt (applied past its evidence); 4 hurt; 5 n/a; 6 met; 7 met; 8 hurt (line passed, not met); 9 hurt; 10 met.
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded:** pre-release research, cluster as one **27 of 39** (individually 28 of 44); coin 26.5 of 40 (28 of 44). Brier running: 39 scored pre-release calls **8.9208 vs the coin's 9.750** (mean 0.2287 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 23 of 32; UP calls 2 of 7; post-release class 1 of 2. Inside-tape good prints: 19 sold, 1 bought (hynix); the disproof condition (two bought in five) is half met.
- **Pending:** STX, HOOD, GEV on the Oct 28 close (wake 21:40 UTC).

## 2026-10-28 (evening) — STX, HOOD (Oct 28 close vs Oct 27; after-close prints), GEV (Oct 28 close vs Oct 27; pre-open print); settled stockanalysis rows, Nasdaq 4 pm prints agree

Written 2026-10-28 21:45 UTC. **STX: DOWN 5.0 at 0.42, realised +4.51 → MISS (Brier 0.3364). HOOD: DOWN 4.0 at 0.33, realised −3.31 → HIT (0.1089). GEV: DOWN 4.5 at 0.35, realised −1.90 on the settled row → HIT (0.1225).** 2 of 3; coins 1 of 3. Cost: one price pull, three exhibits, no agents, no searches.

**The lessons first.**
1. *The regime's disproof condition is met.* Of the last five good prints inside the AI-capex tape — WDC (−3.1), GLW (−1.1), SK hynix (+10.9), STX (+4.5), GEV (−1.9) — two were bought. By the rule I wrote on Oct 15 and restated on Oct 22, the DOWN-on-good-print type resets to **untested** and the UP type to **watch**. The record stands at 19 sold, 2 bought, 21 prints; the type that produced 18 of 20 is no longer the type in force. What replaces it is a split by cell, below.
2. *The active ingredient is the own cell's lopsidedness, not the drawdown.* The hynix amendment (post-drawdown + bouncing tape → own cell) was pre-registered against STX and GEV. STX (own cell 7 of 9 up, 41 % below its high) was bought; GEV (own cell 6 of 9, no lean, 25 % below) was sold small while its whole cell rose. Two lopsided-up memory names beat the regime; a no-lean AI-power name followed it. **Rule in force:** inside the tape, a lopsided own cell (≥ 7 of 9 one way) governs the side after a ≥ 25 % drawdown; a no-lean cell leaves the regime's shape (sold, small) in place; at or near highs the regime governs regardless (tonight's hyperscaler prints test this: MSFT +5.6 % and GOOGL −7.3 % after hours).
3. *Day one's open is the sell; day one's close is the buy.* STX opened −3.2 %, printed −6.9 % at the low and closed +4.5 %. GEV opened −2.2 %, printed −6.6 % and closed −1.9 %. HOOD opened −5.6 % and closed −3.3 %. WDC a week ago sold on the close and recovered over three sessions; today the same digestion fit inside one session. Standing lesson 5 ("day one is not the digestion") inverts for this tape: the digestion now happens before the close, so the call must be about the close and the gap tells less than it did (STX broke a 9-of-9 gap-equals-close record). Size lines built on the day-one sell (Lam/Bloom amendment) have now lost three times in a row on bounce days; **in a bouncing tape size at the mixture median or the shrink line.**
4. *The own-habit rule outside the tape is 2 of 3.* Robinhood sold a record quarter and a tripled October deposit number because half the deposits were a one-off and crypto revenue was −55 %; the habit (9 of 10 down) carried it, as AXP's did; VZ remains the miss. The between-size (mixture median to conditional) landed within 0.7.
5. *First-reporter effect, AI-power pair: 0 of 1.* Vertiv +16.5 % did not lift GE Vernova (−1.9 %). The UPS → FDX pair is tonight's second test. Hypothesis stays open; do not use it as a side-setter until it has a record.
6. *Sizing on bounce days.* On all three names the conditional/options lines (5-7 %) matched the intraday range and overshot the close; the mixture median was nearest twice. The hynix size lesson (size off the live sigma) is about tails on lopsided-up names; it does not override the bounce-day rule in lesson 3 for the called side.

- **Standing lessons marked (STX / HOOD / GEV):** 1 helped ×3; 2 hurt / helped / half; 3 hurt (condition met) / n/a / helped small; 4 helped on |r| / helped / hurt; 5 inverted / n/a / extended; 6-7 met ×3 (GEV reference moved 0.06 % to the settled row); 8 helped in reverse / helped / n/a; 9 hurt / helped / agreed; 10 met ×3.

## 2026-10-29 — Samsung Electronics (KRX close vs Oct 28; pre-open Seoul full results; Daum and stockanalysis rows agree)

Written 2026-10-29 07:30 UTC. **Samsung: DOWN 3.0 % at P(up) 0.42, realised −4.13 % → HIT (size error 1.13), coin DOWN hit, Brier 0.1764.** KOSPI −1.24 %, SK hynix +2.62 %. Cost: three price pulls, one newsroom read, one web search (nothing current).

**The lessons first.**
1. *The second reporter is sold: 2 of 2.* Vertiv +16.5 → GE Vernova −1.9 on Oct 28; SK hynix +10.9 → Samsung −4.1 on Oct 29, with hynix itself up 2.6 % on Samsung's day. In both pairs the first print's bid was taken out of the second name on its own morning, whatever the second name printed. The UPS → FedEx pair grades tonight as the third test. Rule under test: when a close sibling has printed and been bought within the prior two sessions, the second reporter's side leans DOWN and the first reporter's move is the bar, not the street's number.
2. *The bar is the sibling's structure, not the rumour's number.* Samsung announced a ₩30 T dividend — the largest in its history — and a ₩90-110 T 2026 frame, and was sold because hynix had set the structure 24 hours earlier: a 50 %-of-FCF floor, quarterly buybacks, specifics on the day. Samsung matched the total and deferred the specifics to January; "as much, later" read as less. The hynix lesson (a pre-announced number is a bar only when the company lands on it) gains its pair: landing on a sibling's number without the sibling's structure is a met line at best.
3. *The own cell inside the tape is 3 of 3 when lopsided.* After the regime reset, hynix (10 of 12 up) and STX (7 of 9 up) were bought and Samsung's full-results cell (4 of 12 up) was sold, each as its own cell said. GE Vernova (6 of 9, no lean) followed the regime's shrinking sell. The rule in force stands: a lopsided own cell (≥ 7 of 9 one way, or ≤ 4 of 12) governs after a ≥ 25 % drawdown; a no-lean cell leaves the regime's shape. Tonight's hyperscalers test the "at or near highs" clause (MSFT +5.6 % and GOOGL −7.3 % after hours were already pointing opposite ways).
4. *Size for post-drawdown Korean names: split the cell and the live sigma.* The cell said 1.4, the live sigma 5.5, the realised 4.1; the split (3.0) was nearest and the error 1.1. Keep it.
5. *Digestion inside the session, third day running.* Open −0.4 %, high +2.9 % on the dividend headline, low −6.6 %, close −4.1 %. The gap and the first hour are no longer the day; the call must be about the close, and the day-of flip tables keyed on the open are retired for this tape.

- **Standing lessons marked:** 1 helped; 2 helped; 3 n/a; 4 helped; 5 inverted; 6 met; 7 met; 8 helped; 9 helped; 10 met.

## 2026-10-29 (afternoon) — VOW3 (Xetra close vs Oct 28) and SHEL (London close vs Oct 28); pre-open European prints; settled stockanalysis rows

Written 2026-10-29 17:25 UTC. **VOW3: DOWN 2.0 % at P(up) 0.42, realised +1.68 % → MISS (Brier 0.3364). SHEL: UP 2.0 % at P(up) 0.55, realised +2.06 % → HIT (size error 0.06, Brier 0.2025).** 1 of 2; coins 0 of 2. Cost: one price pull, two filings, two searches (nothing current).

**The lessons first.**
1. *The threshold habit rule is 3 of 4, and it works in both directions.* Shell's 7-of-8 up habit overrode a record-high chased base and the stock closed +2.1 % on a $5 B buyback; AXP and HOOD's 1-of-9 habits overrode de-rated bases downward; VZ's 7-of-8 habit lost to a dated overhang. Rule in force unchanged: ≥ 7 of 8 one way governs over the base classification unless a dated overhang points the other way.
2. *Passing the bar is the positive case of "a bar only if landed on".* Shell's crowd was anchored on a $3 B quarterly buyback after the ARC pause; the company announced $5 B and the stock was bought. hynix (₩16 T rumoured, ₩100 T delivered) was the same case; Samsung (matched hynix's total, deferred the structure) and HSBC ($1 B expected, $1 B delivered) were the met-line cases, sold. The update-note operating lines were, as predicted, zero information: the earnings beat moved nothing the buyback sentence had not already moved.
3. *European cyclicals on a sector day trade the sector.* Volkswagen printed the met-line quarter I described (charges inside the flagged €8 B, underlying margin 3.8 %, guidance held), opened −1.4 % as its cell said, and closed +1.7 % with Porsche +3.2 %, Mercedes +2.4 % and the DAX +1.4 %. The pre-registered test (own cell vs base classification on a de-rated non-AI name) went to the base classification, 1 of 1, and the one unguided positive line (9M net cash flow €4.4 B) was bought — lesson 8 running the buyers' way for the fourth time (PYPL, UPS, NOVN, VOW3). European own-cell rule: 3 of 5 (ASML, HSBC, SHEL hits; NOVN, VOW3 misses — both misses were cells overriding a de-rated base). Amendment: on European names the own cell governs **unless** the base is de-rated (≥ 25 % below the high) and the cell is not lopsided; then the base classification (UP) governs, sized at the shrink line because the sector day decides the magnitude.
4. *Digestion inside the session, fifth day running.* VOW3 −1.4 → +1.7; SHEL +1.9 → +3.9 → +2.1. The gap is no longer the day on either side of the Atlantic.
5. *UP calls are 3 of 8.* The three hits (BLK, SPOT post-release class aside, SHEL) share a lopsided-up own habit; the misses (GS, C, MS, VZ, KO) share a habit under threshold or an overhang. The UP rule's own-habit requirement is doing its job; the cost is coin-flip names like Merck where the lowered bar leans up and the rule forbids the call — graded tonight.

- **Standing lessons marked (VOW3 / SHEL):** 1 helped ×2; 2 hurt / helped; 3 n/a; 4 helped on |r| / helped; 5 inverted ×2; 6-7 met ×2; 8 hurt (unguided positive bought) / helped; 9 own cell overrode the base, miss / own cell governed, hit; 10 met ×2.

## 2026-10-29 (evening) — MSFT, GOOGL, NOW, CMG, EBAY, FDX (Oct 29 close vs Oct 28; after-close prints) and CAT, MA, MRK, LLY (Oct 29 close vs Oct 28; pre-open prints); Nasdaq 4 pm prints; settled stockanalysis rows confirmed at 21:40 UTC for eight, GOOGL (282.69) and LLY (1,365.40) rows disagree with the Nasdaq closes by 0.4-0.55 %, signs unaffected, re-check Oct 30

Written 2026-10-29 21:20 UTC, graded early at the user's note that all ten had closed. **Research 4 of 10; the coin 6 of 10. Brier for the night 2.6410 over ten calls (mean 0.264) against the coin's 0.250 — my first graded round worse than the coin on both counts.** After-close six: MSFT −0.16 HIT (by a hair), GOOGL −12.64 HIT, NOW −26.97 HIT, CMG +8.86 HIT, EBAY +2.69 MISS, FDX +1.06 MISS (4 of 6). Pre-open four: CAT −9.27 MISS, MA +4.18 MISS, MRK +3.85 MISS, LLY +6.57 MISS (0 of 4). Reference corrections: NOW 150.13, CMG 28.73 and FDX 304.58 are the settled Oct 28 closes, not the 4:04 pm prints I recorded (closing auctions); none changes a sign. Cost: one quote pull, one history pull, ten filings, five searches.

**The lessons first.**
1. *Outside the AI tape the guide table governs, not the base. The chased-base rule is retired.* EBAY (passed its own EPS range, FY GMV raised), MA (revenue +14 % against a +12 % bar), LLY (a $5.5 B raise) and FDX (CY26 EPS raised) were all bought from bases I had classified as chased or neutral; MRK (FY raised at the midpoint) was bought from a borderline base. Raised or beaten tables outside the tape are now bought 13 of 13 since the Oct 22 reset (SPCX, IBM, LMT, SPOT, PYPL, UPS, SHEL, CMG, EBAY, FDX, MA, MRK, LLY); the sold cases outside the tape (AXP, VZ, KO, BA, HSBC, Samsung, NOVN's small up) were a lopsided-down habit, a dated overhang, a defensive on a sector day, quantified misses, or met pre-released lines. Rule in force from tonight: **outside the tape, classify the table first — raised or beaten above the whole range → UP whatever the base (size at the own up-median or gap × retention); met or landed-on → the base and the own habit decide, sized small; cut → DOWN. A chased base only sells a met print.** This is uncomfortably close to the pretraining prior I was told not to trust; the record says the prior is right outside the tape and wrong inside it, and the asset is knowing which side of the line a name is on.
2. *Inside the AI tape the regime is intact, and the lopsided-cell override needs a raised table.* MSFT printed the best Azure quarter in years (48 %, guided 49-50 %), was bought +5.6 % after hours and gave all of it back inside the session; GOOGL was sold −12.6 % on 2027 capex, negative FCF and a mark-driven miss with Cloud at +103 %; NOW was sold −27 % on a cRPO guide 1.3 points under the street; CAT was sold −9.3 % on a held guide and a low-end margin frame with Power Generation at +30 %. DOWN-on-good-print inside the tape is 25 of 30 (the hynix and STX breaks were followed by four straight sells). The lopsided-cell rule (own cell governs after a ≥ 25 % drawdown) is 3 of 4: hynix, STX and Samsung had passed or missed a pre-announced bar; CAT held its table and was sold like GE Vernova. Amendment: **inside the tape a held table is sold whatever the cell; the cell overrides only when the table is raised or a bar is passed.**
3. *The UP rule's own-habit requirement cost two hits and is amended.* MRK was the pre-registered case: a street-lowered bar (ten cuts in 30 days, the stock −6 % into the print) cleared with the FY raised, bought +3.9 %, and my framework forbade the call. The street-lowered-bar pattern is 2 of 2 (NOVN, MRK), both missed. LLY's 4-of-4 recent habit under the 7-of-8 threshold pointed up and was overridden by the retired chased rule. Amendment: **outside the tape an UP call is allowed on a raised table, on a street-lowered bar cleared, or on a ≥ 7-of-8 habit, with no dated overhang; the own habit is no longer required when the table is raised.**
4. *Size: the tails were the lines tonight.* GOOGL −12.6 against a 3.5 call (the C down leg, −8, was closest), NOW −27 against 6.0 (the C leg, −15, closest), CMG +8.9 against 3.5 (gap × retention 10.8 closest), CAT −9.3 beyond even the C leg, MSFT −0.16 against 4.0 (no-change line). Amendments: on a bimodal name inside the tape whose last three prints were sold, size at the C scenario, not the live sigma or the mixture (NOW); UP calls on de-rated names size at gap × retention when the after-hours gap is ≥ +5 %, else the up-median (CMG, SHEL); a hyperscaler at the highs with its capex guide lowered is a no-change print — size at the shrink line, not the conditional (MSFT).
5. *Second-reporter rule is 2 of 3; seasonal cells are retired.* UPS→FDX went up/up: the sector bar rose and FedEx cleared it, so the rule's "sibling's move is the bar" clause was right and the DOWN lean was wrong — amend to: the second reporter is bought if it clears the sibling's bar (raises as the sibling did) and sold if it merely matches (GEV, Samsung). V→MA (sibling flat) carried no information. GEV→CAT was the same cell selling twice. The Octobers-0-of-2 cells (CMG, EBAY, MA, MRK) went 0 of 4 as a predictor and are retired: a two-observation seasonal cell is noise.
6. *Lesson 8 (the unquantified line is sold) has a buyers' side and a failure case.* Tonight the unquantified positive lines were bought on CMG (traffic), EBAY (GMV acceleration), MA (services), MRK (Winrevair, Gardasil), FDX (the raise) — six of the last seven outside-tape prints — and the quantified negative line on LLY (US price −7 %) was ignored because volume +53 % swamped it. Restated: the line the crowd grades is the one that is new relative to the table; a quantified negative is sold only when it is the only new line (VZ, HSBC).
7. *Digestion inside the session, sixth day running, with one new shape.* MSFT's +1.7 % gap reversed to a down close — the first hyperscaler after-close gap to reverse in this ledger; FDX's −1.0 % gap reversed to an up close; EBAY's +3.3 % half-faded. The extended gaps (GOOGL, NOW, CMG, CAT, MA, MRK, LLY) were all A- or C-scenario prints. Hypothesis to test: the gap holds when the print is a scenario tail and is digested when the print is the mixture.
8. *Two bar errors to record against lesson 7.* NOW's EPS bar (0.56) was on the wrong share basis; CAT's $72 B backlog bar was an S-tagged number the release does not support. Neither set a side; both would have been caught by reading the prior exhibit's share count and backlog line. Rule: a bar is a bar only when it is read off the company's last exhibit on the same basis.

- **Standing lessons marked (ten calls):** 1 helped ×4 / hurt ×6; 2 helped ×4 / hurt ×6; 3 n/a; 4 hurt ×5 (GOOGL, NOW, MSFT, CMG, CAT) / helped ×1 (FDX size); 5 inverted ×3 (MSFT, FDX, EBAY) / held ×7; 6 met ×10; 7 met ×8, weak ×2 (NOW basis, CAT backlog); 8 helped ×3 (GOOGL, NOW, CAT sellers' side), helped-buyers ×5, hurt ×1 (LLY), n/a ×1; 9 regime governed and hit ×3 (MSFT, GOOGL, NOW), base classification hit ×1 (CMG), base rule overrode the habit and lost ×3 (EBAY, MA, LLY), own cell overrode the regime and lost ×1 (CAT), cells set the side against a lowered bar and lost ×1 (MRK), neutral lean lost ×1 (FDX); 10 met ×10.
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded, the ten counted individually:** pre-release research **35 of 55** (individually 36 of 60); coin 34.5 of 56 (36 of 60). Brier running: 55 scored pre-release calls **12.8449 vs the coin's 13.750** (mean 0.2336 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 29 of 45; UP calls 4 of 10; post-release class 1 of 2. Coin-distance: calls at P(up) ≤ 0.42 went 11 of 14 (MSFT, NOW hits; EBAY, MA misses tonight); calls within 0.05 of the coin went 7 of 15 (CMG hit; FDX, CAT, MRK, LLY misses).
- **Pending:** INTC, AMZN, SBUX, RDDT, SNDK, COIN, MSTR, CVX on the Oct 30 close (wake Oct 30 21:00 UTC). The SBUX (second reporter after CMG, chased) and CVX (second reporter after SHEL, chased) locks were made under rules amended tonight; they stand as locked and are graded as tests of the old rules.

## 2026-10-30 (London) — AZN (London close vs Oct 29; pre-open 07:00 GMT print); stockanalysis lon-azn row read after the close

Written 2026-10-30 17:15 UTC. **AZN: UP 2.0 % at P(up) 0.58, realised +6.96 % → HIT (Brier 0.1764), the largest AstraZeneca results-day move in sixteen prints; the coin (DOWN) missed.** Cost: one history pull, one quote pull, one filing.

**The lessons first.**
1. *A company-lowered bar cleared is a raise in the crowd's accounting.* AstraZeneca reconfirmed its FY table and was bought 7 % because the two lines it had flagged lower for H2 — core gross margin and finance costs — came in at 82 % (flat to +1 pt) and +27 % respectively, with Core EPS 7.5 % over the bar and operating margin up 4 points. The street-lowered or company-lowered bar pattern is **3 of 3 (NOVN +1.0, MRK +3.9, AZN +7.0)** and this was the first call made with it after the Oct 29 amendment. Rule in force: outside the AI tape, a pre-flagged negative line that does not land is up-side information of the same rank as a guide raise; "the table governs" reads the pre-flagged lines as part of the table.
2. *The amended second-reporter rule needs its "matches" clause read against the open lines, not the guide.* I named the +2.3 % sympathy bid of Oct 29 as the risk and wrote that a merely-matching print gives it back. The FY table merely matched; the open lines (EPS, margin) cleared the siblings' bar, and the bid was extended, not returned. Record: VRT→GEV sold (matched), hynix→Samsung sold (matched), UPS→FDX bought (cleared with a raise), LLY/MRK→AZN bought (cleared on the open lines). 3 of 4 as a rule once "clears" includes a cleared lowered bar.
3. *European own-cell rule is 4 of 6; the lopsided-up cell on a neutral base is the clean case.* ASML, HSBC, SHEL, AZN hits (cells of 7-8 of 8); NOVN and VOW3 misses (cells overriding a de-rated base). The Oct 29 European amendment (de-rated + non-lopsided → base governs) stands untested since VOW3.
4. *Size on a lowered bar cleared: the up-tail, not the median.* MRK printed 2× its up-median, AZN 2.8×; the A-scenario up leg (3.5) and gap × retention (5.3) were the only lines within 3.5 points. Amendment to the UP sizing rule: when the configuration is a lowered bar cleared or a lopsided-up cell with a company-flagged negative line, size at the A-scenario up leg; the own up-median applies only to a met table on an unflagged print (SHEL).
5. *Digestion inside the session, seventh day, on the buyers' side this time.* Gap +3.0 → high +7.3 → close +7.0: an up-gap on a lopsided-up name extended (10 of 13 for AstraZeneca). The hypothesis from Oct 29 — the gap holds when the print is a scenario tail and is digested when it is the mixture — gets a supporting case.

- **Standing lessons marked (AZN):** 1 helped; 2 helped; 3 n/a; 4 hurt; 5 held; 6 met; 7 met with the China Q3 cell unread; 8 helped (buyers' side); 9 own cell governed, hit; 10 met.
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded:** pre-release research **36 of 56** (individually 37 of 61); coin 34.5 of 57. Brier running: 56 scored pre-release calls **13.0213 vs the coin's 14.000** (mean 0.2325 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 29 of 45; **UP calls 5 of 11**; post-release class 1 of 2.

## 2026-10-30 — INTC, AMZN, SBUX, RDDT, SNDK, COIN, MSTR (Oct 30 close vs Oct 29; after-close prints) and CVX, XOM (Oct 30 close vs Oct 29; pre-open prints); Nasdaq 4 pm prints, settled rows to confirm

Written 2026-10-30 21:20 UTC, graded at the user's note that the market had closed. **Research 6 of 9; the coin 2 of 9. Brier for the day 2.0302 over nine calls (mean 0.226) against the coin's 0.250.** INTC −28.58 HIT, AMZN −1.41 HIT, SBUX −1.76 HIT, RDDT −29.27 MISS, SNDK −13.47 HIT, COIN −10.62 HIT, MSTR −7.84 HIT, CVX +1.76 MISS, XOM +2.17 MISS. The day was a liquidation (SPY −1.8 %, QQQ −3.5 %, SOXX −8.2 %, AMD −10.9 % without a print, bitcoin −5 %) with the energy sector bid +1.9 % on crude; five of the six hits were sold with the tape, and I do not claim the magnitudes. Reference corrections: RDDT 129.60 (settled, not the 129.90 print); CVX 231.46 (a tick). LLY's Oct 29 close settled at 1,373.70 (realised +6.63, from +6.57); GOOGL's 283.82 confirmed. Cost: one quote pull, one history pull, nine filings, no searches.

**The lessons first.**
1. *The base-classification UP rule does not apply to a growth name whose growth line is in question.* Reddit beat its revenue guide, guided Q4 above $1 B and fell 29 % because US daily actives were −2 % and total actives flat quarter on quarter. I had that exact line as my C scenario at 15 % and let a 51 % drawdown override it; a de-rating is not a floor when the user line turns, because the de-rating was the market pricing the user line. The rule's record as UP calls is 1 of 2 (CMG, RDDT). Amendment: **the de-rated-base UP applies only when every line of the table is intact; a live C-scenario line on users, volumes or the guide outranks the base, and a growth name with a user line in question is not an UP candidate at any drawdown.**
2. *Inside the AI tape the regime held through a liquidation, and the A prints were sold hardest.* Sandisk (beat, raise, buyback, split) −13.5 %; Intel (beat on every line, Q4 guide down 6 % q/q) −28.6 %; Amazon (AWS +39 %, Q4 guide $240-247 B, TTM FCF −$42 B) −10 % after hours bought back to −1.4 %. Inside-tape good prints are now 27 of 32 sold; the only hyperscaler whose capex sell was bought back inside the day was the one with the cloud acceleration (AWS) and a guide $20 B above the street — the cloud line sets the size, the capex line the sign (4 of 4).
3. *On a name whose print-day sign follows its sector 9 or 10 times in 10, the sector's pre-market sign at lock outranks every other rule.* Chevron (XLE-sign 10 of 11) and Exxon (print-day underperformance vs XLE 4 of 4, broken today) were both bought with a +2 % energy bid that was visible before either print and held on a −1.8 % market day. I ranked that fact below the second-reporter rule, the retired chased-base rule and the lopsided own cell, and lost twice. Rule: **when the sector is bid ≥ 1.5 % pre-market at lock and the name's sector-sign dependence is ≥ 9 of 10, the side is the sector's or there is no call.** The threshold-habit rule is 4 of 6 (both misses, VZ and XOM, lost to a sector-scale force).
4. *The second-reporter rule is retired as a separate rule; it was the table rule wearing a different name.* Original reading (sibling bought → second leans DOWN): 4 of 8 — a coin (GEV, Samsung, SBUX, SNDK sold; FDX, CVX, XOM, AZN bought). Amended reading (clears the sibling's bar → bought, matches → sold): 6 of 8, with SBUX and SNDK the exceptions, both confounded (a comp-guide deceleration; a liquidation day). "Clears vs matches" is exactly what the Oct 29 table rule says. Fold it in and stop counting sibling order.
5. *Size: on a tail print or a liquidation day the C scenario is the line.* INTC (C −14 vs −28.6), RDDT (C −15 vs −29.3), SNDK (C −12 vs −13.5), COIN (C −12 / median −8 vs −10.6), MSTR (C −8 vs −7.8) — the C leg was the closest or only adequate line on five of six sold prints; the mixture won only on Amazon, where the after-hours sell was bought back. Rule in force: **when the print lands in the C scenario, or the tape is a liquidation (index ≥ 1.5 % down, sector ETF ≥ 3 % down), size at the C leg; the mixture applies to in-line prints on a calm tape; the shrink line is for the no-change case (MSFT).** The Oct 28 "bounce-day" shrink rule is retired — it has never had a bounce day to work on.
6. *Three bar errors against lesson 7.* Amazon's Q4 street line ($222 B) was an S inference wrong by $20 B; Starbucks' table had no comp-guide line, and the comp guide (4 % vs a 7-8 % run-rate) set the sign; Chevron's buyback cell was lost in extraction. None changed a side. The rule from last night stands: a bar is a bar only when read off the company's last exhibit on the same basis, and the guide table must carry every line the company habitually guides.
7. *Digestion: tails hold, mixtures digest — five more cases.* After-hours or opening gaps extended on INTC, RDDT, SNDK, COIN, MSTR (all tail prints); Amazon's −10 % after-hours print (an in-line table with a capex sell) was digested to −1.4 %; Starbucks' +0.7 % high was faded. The hypothesis written on Oct 29 is now 12 of 13 (AZN included); it is a rule for sizing the gap-to-close leg, not for the side.

- **Standing lessons marked (nine calls):** 1 helped ×6 / hurt ×3; 2 helped ×6 / hurt ×3; 3 helped ×3 (INTC, SNDK, AMZN regime), n/a ×6; 4 hurt ×7 (INTC, AMZN, RDDT, SNDK, COIN, MSTR, XOM) / helped ×2 (SBUX, CVX size); 5 held ×7, inverted ×2 (AMZN, SBUX); 6 met ×9; 7 met ×6, weak ×3 (AMZN, SBUX, CVX); 8 helped ×5 (INTC, AMZN, SBUX, MSTR, RDDT-against-me), n/a ×4; 9 regime/own-cell agreed and hit ×5, base rule overrode a live C line and lost ×1 (RDDT), cross-name rules outranked the sector sign and lost ×2 (CVX, XOM), rules hit for an unstated reason ×1 (SBUX); 10 met ×9.
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded, counted individually:** pre-release research **42 of 65** (individually 43 of 70); coin 36.5 of 66. ## 2026-11-03 — AAPL and PLTR (Nov 3 close vs Nov 2; after-close prints Nov 2; two calls each: Opus 5.5 lock, Fable 5.1 re-call), PFE and SHOP (Nov 3 close vs Nov 2; pre-open prints; v1 lock, v2 after a fresh read of this file); closes PROVISIONAL (settled rows not posted; the quote sites served this container stale pages), re-stamp Nov 4

Written 2026-11-03 21:50 UTC by the session that made the calls. Full grade: `GRADE_2026-11-03.md`; process: `GRADING_PROTOCOL.md` (new tonight). **AAPL DOWN 2.5 / 3.0 at P(up) 0.42 / 0.40 → −1.90 % (settled 331.90, re-stamped Nov 4), HIT, right reason (December GM guide 45-46 % fired KS1; Services +10 % fired KS4). PLTR UP 8 / 10 at 0.53 / 0.52 → −1.65 % (4 pm print 175.40 vs 178.34), MISS, reaction miss (KS1 and KS3 fired: revenue $2.313 B +96 %, FY raised to $8.53 B; sold on TCV +6 % from +49 %, US commercial TCV +11 % from +153 %, RPO flat). PFE UP 1.5 at 0.55 / 0.53 → −2.3 % (29.98, MarketBeat close), MISS, reaction miss (EPS $0.87 vs $0.73, both FY lines raised, KS1 and KS3 fired). SHOP DOWN 8 at 0.42 / 0.44 → **−8.74 %** (settled 142.57, re-stamped Nov 4; the −5.65 % graded last night was a mid-session figure), HIT, table right / mechanism wrong (revenue $3.742 B in line, GMV and subscription light → the table's C leg; the Q4 guide held at "low 30s", so the named sign-setter did not fire).** Pairs counted as one: 2 of 4; coin 1 of 4 (AAPL, PLTR, PFE coins UP, SHOP DOWN); Brier 0.9202 vs 1.000. Cost: one grading pass, no agents, but ~40 fetches hunting closes.

**The lessons first.**
1. *The forward line for a bookings-driven software name is bookings, and the trigger is deceleration, not sign.* Palantir beat and raised every guided line and was sold on TCV growth falling from +49 % to +6 % and US commercial TCV from +153 % to +11 %, with RPO flat q/q and large deals 64 vs 73. KS2 asked whether US commercial TCV was *down* y/y; the crowd asked whether it had decelerated by more than half. Rule: on names above ~30× sales carry a switch "bookings growth (TCV or RPO) below half the prior quarter's rate" at P ≥ 0.30 mapped to C, outranking the revenue beat. Record this fortnight for growth names sold on a decelerating non-revenue line: NOW (cRPO), RDDT (users), PLTR (TCV) — 3 of 3. The rubric's reading "AI software trades like the capex tape" is **not** confirmed: the close was −1.65 % after a −8 % first scan, a digestion, not a capex-tape sell. Disproof: two of the next five such prints close up ≥ +2 %.
2. *Defensive at its high after a run-in outranks the outside-tape table rule.* Pfizer: +21.5 % in three months, within 2 % of the 52-week high, a 19 % EPS beat, both FY lines raised, closed down ~2 %. The Oct 27 rule ("no UP calls on defensives at highs; coin and small") was in force; I ranked the Oct 29 table rule above it because the table was raised. Hierarchy from tonight: inside the tape → regime; outside → (a) dated overhang, (b) defensive at highs with d63 ≥ +15 % → no UP call, (c) the table rule. The outside-tape raised-table record goes **13 of 14** (PFE the one sold), and the exception has a configuration. Defensives at highs on raised or beaten tables: PM −1.2, KO −1.6, NOVN +1.0, PFE −2.2 → 1 of 4 up. Disproof: two of the next four close up ≥ +2 %.
3. *The first scan sets the day's extreme, not the close — 3 of 3 today, 7 of the last 9 negative first scans.* AAPL −4.4 → −1.9; PLTR −8 → −1.65; SHOP −16 → −8.74 (re-stamped; earlier: AMZN −10 → −1.4, STX −6.9 → +4.5, GEV −6.6 → −1.9, GLW −4.7 → −1.1; INTC and RDDT extended on a liquidation day). The "tails hold, mixtures digest" hypothesis (12 of 13) is marked **hurt** on SHOP, a tail-sized scan digested by about 45 % on a bounce day (re-stamped from the wrong −5.65: the digestion was smaller than written last night, so the lesson's 0.3-0.5× range holds at its upper edge, 0.55×), and narrows to "tails hold on liquidation days": now 12 of 14. Sizing rule to test: for a DOWN call on an after-close or pre-open print, the close lands at 0.3-0.5× the first scan unless the print is a C tail on a liquidation day; record first-scan × 0.4 beside the mixture and the conditional. Disproof: three of the next five negative first scans ≥ 4 % extend into the close.
4. *A table can be right while the governing sentence is wrong, and the rubric must say so.* SHOP's C leg named the revenue shortfall; the governing sentence named a decelerating Q4 guide (P 0.60 in v2) that did not come. "Luck" is the pre-registered word and too blunt. `GRADING_PROTOCOL.md` adds a fifth outcome, **table right / mechanism wrong**: a hit on the sheet, a half-miss on the mechanism series.
5. *Premise errors are found by re-reading the bar, not the lessons.* The AMD v1/v2 docs carried a wrong drawdown (11 % vs 29 %) and a wrong Q4 bar ($14.3-15.0 B vs the street's $16.1-16.3 B), caught only at the v3 re-call; the rule "a bar is a bar only when read off the company's last exhibit on the same basis" existed and was not followed. The protocol now requires the source of every switch threshold in the doc. AMD printed Q4 "approximately $17 B ± 3 %" and GM "approximately 55 %" tonight (M); graded Nov 4.
6. *UP calls are 5 of 14.* Tonight's two UP calls both lost on raised tables: one to a bookings line (PLTR), one to the defensive-at-highs configuration (PFE). Both DOWN calls hit, both at or under the options size. The lean of the record is still the regime, not a bias, but the UP gate tightens by lessons 1 and 2.
7. *Non-independent pairs.* Two models or two versions on one print from one session are one view; graded separately on the sheet, counted once in the tallies (mean P(up), mean size), per the Oct 13 bank-cluster convention. Within-pair comparison tonight: the lower P(up) went the right way on PLTR and PFE and the wrong way on SHOP; on AAPL the smaller size (2.5) was nearer. Meaningless at n = 1 per pair; recorded for the series.
8. *Data rule for this container.* Quote and history sites (stockanalysis, Yahoo, MarketScreener, Investing.com, TipRanks) returned pages days to months stale through the fetch tool all evening; the only same-day closes came from press quote boxes ("as of 04:00 PM Eastern"). Take the first same-day press close as provisional, stamp it, and stop; re-stamp from the settled row the next morning.

- **Standing lessons marked (AAPL / PLTR / PFE / SHOP):** 1 (cost) met ×4; 2 (digestion over data) helped / helped / helped / helped; 3 (sold-print regime) n/a (none inside the tape); 4 (size) helped (between-size) / moot / moot (shrink line right in magnitude) / helped (conditional 8 vs 8.74 re-stamped: the closest line; last night's "mixture closer" rested on the wrong close); 5 (day one) all three negative first scans digested; 6-7 met, closes provisional; 8 (forward line) helped (GM guide) / helped in a form not carried (bookings) / neutral / hurt (guide held); 9 (own cell vs rule) agreed / table rule lost to a bookings line / Oct 27 defensive rule **not followed**, hurt / own cell agreed; 10 met; Oct 29 table rule hurt ×2 (PLTR, PFE); Oct 27 lesson 3 helped in reverse (PFE); "tails hold" hurt (SHOP).
- **Tallies (D), SAP excluded, pairs as one, closes provisional:** pre-release research **44 of 69** (individually 47 of 78); coin 37.5 of 70. ## 2026-11-04 — BMW (Xetra close vs Nov 3) and NVO (Copenhagen close vs Nov 3); pre-open European prints; BMW settled, NVO provisional (DKK close not served; EUR Lang & Schwarz prints)

Written 2026-11-04 17:50 UTC by the session that made both calls. Full grade: `GRADE_2026-11-04_EU.md`. **BMW DOWN 4.5 at P(up) 0.27 → +5.50 % (57.80 → 60.98 settled), MISS, reaction miss, coin UP hit, Brier 0.5329. NVO DOWN 4.0 at 0.41 → about +5.5 % (provisional), MISS, fundamentals miss, coin DOWN miss, Brier 0.3481.** 0 of 2; coin 1 of 2. Tape: DAX +2.5 to +2.8 % on the US midterm result; both names ran ~3 points past it. Cost: one grading pass, no agents, ~45 fetches (Copenhagen close still not served at 17:45 UTC).

**The lessons first.**
1. *A warning is a lowered bar, not a regime (BMW).* The Nov 3 warning was in the reference; the Nov 4 print was graded against the corridor the warning set, and the Q3 Automotive margin of 3.4 % (4.5 % ex-PPA) against a −0.5/+0.5 % FY corridor and a 2.3 % Q2 was a beat. The unquantified Q4 charge (my KS2 C-leg) and the −€0.6 B Q3 Automotive FCF (KS3 down-leg) were ignored; "margin beats guidance" was the headline and the stock ran +6.8 % from a −1.2 % open. The warning-day analogs (June −7, Mercedes −3.9) describe warning days, not the results day after one. Rule: on a post-warning results print, a quarter line above the lowered corridor is scenario A at P(up|A) ≥ 0.60 whatever the charge detail. Record for the warning-cell reading: 0 of 1. Disproof: the next post-warning results print with an operating-line beat closes ≤ −2 %.
2. *The lowered-bar-cleared configuration is 5 of 5 as a pattern and 1 of 5 as calls.* NOVN +1.0, MRK +3.9, AZN +7.0, NVO +5.57 (DKK 285.20, re-stamped Nov 5), BMW +5.5. Both docs today named the configuration in the alternative paragraph (NVO: "UP at the A up-leg +5" — the close landed on it) and ranked a sold-analog above it (the Aug "fell short of hopes" template; the warning cell). Rule in force: a company- or street-lowered bar cleared on the headline line is UP at the A up-leg, and no met-line analog (Aug template, warning cell, buyback-size cell, 9M-narrowing habit) outranks it. Disproof: two of the next four cleared lowered bars close ≤ −1 %.
3. *European own-cell rule is 4 of 7; the lopsided-down clause failed its first test.* Novo's 1-of-6 cell governed over a de-rated base with a raised table and lost. Amendment: on a ≥ 25 %-de-rated European name a lopsided-down cell governs only on a met or cut table; on a raised table or a cleared lowered bar the table governs (the Oct 29 US table rule, extended to Europe). Disproof: a raised table on such a name closes ≤ −2 % in the next three cases.
4. *A one-observation habit is not a habit.* Novo's "9M habit is to narrow" was n = 1 (Nov 2025) and carried P(B) 0.45; the company raised for the third time. A behaviour cell needs n ≥ 3 to move a scenario weight by more than 0.10 (the Oct 29 seasonal-cell retirement, restated for company habits).
5. *Sector-day term before the open.* DAX +2.5 % on the midterm result was knowable from US futures before 09:00 CET; KS6/KS8 were size-only rows. On a ≥ 2 % index day the tape moves P(up) by +0.05 to +0.10 (VOW3 lesson), not only the size. Disproof: the next European print on a ≥ 2 % index day closes against the index.
6. *The repeated error is the ranking of evidence cells, not the probabilities.* Oct 27 (×4), Oct 28 (×1) and today (×2) share one shape: the doc names the cell that fits the configuration and then ranks a cross-name analog above it. Oct 28 lesson 5 ("name the single evidence cell that fits; override only with evidence from the same configuration") was written in both docs and not followed. Process rule: the governing sentence must cite the cell's n and record; an override needs a cell with n ≥ 3 in the same configuration, or it goes on the sensitivity line.

- **Standing lessons marked (BMW / NVO):** 1 met / met; 2 hurt / hurt; 3 n/a; 4 moot / moot (magnitudes within 1.5 on the wrong side); 5 open −1.2 → close +5.5 (session bought) / open +6.6 → +1.9 → ≈ +5.5 (digested both ways); 6 met ×2; 7 met / provisional; 8 hurt in reverse ×2 (pre-flagged negative beaten; one-observation habit); 9 hurt ×2 (cross-name cell over the fitting cell); 10 met ×2.

Full grade: `GRADE_2026-11-04_US.md`. Cost: eight fetch batches, no exhibits beyond the five releases; the price pull failed on every history site.

**The lessons first.**
1. *The lowered-bar-cleared configuration is 6 of 6 as a pattern (NOVN, MRK, AZN, NVO, BMW, MCD) and 2 of 2 as calls (AZN, MCD).* McDonald's flagged US comps "slightly negative" in August and September, printed −0.2 % with adj EPS 3.57 vs 3.37 from a base 23 % off the high, and was paid +6.5 % (280.22), its largest print move in the ledger. Rule in force: a lowered named line cleared (even by a hair) with the table met or better, on a de-rated base, no dated overhang → UP, **sized at the options event-only line, not the own up-median** (the six prints +1.0/+3.9/+7.0/+5.6/+5.5/+6.5 against own medians of 1.5-2.5 and options lines of 3-5). Disproof: two of the next four such prints close ≤ 0.
2. *A de-rated base buys a met line unless a ≥ 7-of-8 own habit says otherwise; n = 6 is not a habit (UBER).* Uber guided Q4 EBITDA/GB a hair under the street and EPS well under (0.95-0.99 vs 1.06), the modal print I described, and the 52-week-low base paid +7.3 %. The Oct 29 threshold rule (≥ 7 of 8 governs over the base) was not followed: UBER's habit was 5 of 6. De-rated bases outside the tape on met or lowered-bar prints are bought 7 of 7 this fortnight (PYPL, UPS, VOW3, BMW, NVO, UBER, MCD); the sold cases (AXP 1 of 9, HOOD 9 of 10) had threshold habits. Disproof: a de-rated base with a sub-threshold habit sells a met line twice in the next five.
3. *Inside the AI tape a raised table above the street closes up — by 1 % after a 7 % gap (AMD).* The $17 B Q4 guide was 5 % above the street's $16.1-16.3 B with GM ≈ 55 % against a 55.7 % model and a 2027 ">80 % growth" frame on the call; after-hours +7 %, open-hour −2.7 %, afternoon +3 %, **close +1.01 %** (−0.6 relative to the S&P): the MSFT shape (Oct 29), a tail gap digested to ≈ 0 inside the session, and the no-change size line won again (2 of 2 on hyperscaler/GPU raises from a flushed base). The doc's own clause ("only a raise above the street hands the side over") was right at 0.30; it belongs at ≥ 0.40 for a company that has beaten its own guide top 4 of 4 into a cut street bar, and a margin dip inside 100 bp of the model is not a sign-setter when the revenue raise is ≥ 4 %. Regime record: 28 sold of 34 (AMD the fourth break; DDOG's held table sold the same day). Disproof: a ≥ 4 % raise above the street inside the tape closes ≤ 0 twice in the next four.
4. *Bimodal inside-tape names size at min(conditional, options), not the compromise (DDOG).* Own |r| median 16, options 14.6, conditional 14; I wrote 8. The Oct 29 NOW rule was gated (last print sold −19 at the high) and not followed: 6.3 pt of size error. Disproof: the next two such names close inside the shrink line.
5. *An unregistered corporate action is luck on the mechanism series whatever the rubric's letter says (CVS).* The table went A on all three gates (2027 ≥ $8.65, FY ≥ 8.20, Q3 2.09) and the stock fell 12 % on the split, with insurers +3 to +10 % the same day; KS4 fired C on a quarterly MBR (91.3 %) that was mis-keyed — the FY MBR guide is the bar (a lesson-7 bar error). The call does not count toward any evidence cell.
6. *Brier and hit rate diverged (3 of 5, Brier 0.265).* Both misses sat at P(up) 0.40/0.41 in the ≤ 0.42 band (now 17 of 23) and both were the configuration in lessons 2-3. Number to compute before a DOWN call at ≤ 0.42: is the name ≥ 25 % off its high with a met-or-better modal print? If so the band's record does not apply and P(up) belongs at 0.45-0.55.
7. *Sector-day term, US form.* S&P +1.6 %, Dow +3 % on the midterm sweep; UBER +5.5 and MCD +4 relative, so the prints did most of it, but a ≥ 1.5 % index day should add +0.05 to P(up) on de-rated non-tape names before the open (the BMW/NVO lesson 5, second day running). AMD +4 relative to semis (semis ETF −1 %, NVDA −1.6 %): the print, not the tape.
8. *Data rule.* DDOG's reference is stamped 220.52 (three vendors; the doc's ≈216.11 was wrong by 2 %); MCD's reference moves to 263.04 (the 262.74 was a 3:59 PM print). Re-stamped Nov 5: AMD +1.01 (the +3.1 graded the evening before was a mid-afternoon print — grading a near-flat name on an intraday print cost 2 pt of r; the sign held), CVS −12.60, UBER +7.31, MCD +6.53, NOVO B +5.57 (DKK 285.20); DDOG's Nov 4 row is still not served (≈ −14.3 provisional, sign not in doubt). Rule: on a name whose intraday range straddles zero, do not grade before a dated close is served; wait for the morning row.

## 2026-11-05 (Europe) — DHL (Xetra close vs Nov 4; pre-open print); settled stockanalysis row

Written 2026-11-05 17:20 UTC. **DHL: DOWN 2.0 % at P(up) 0.42, realised +0.20 % → MISS (Brier 0.3364); the coin (UP) hit.** The quarter had been pre-released on Oct 16 with the guidance raise; the Nov 5 statement carried every line unchanged, the one open line (Q3 free cash flow €901 M against a €1.31 B company-compiled consensus) missed, and the stock went −1.7 % at the low to +0.2 % at the close — another record close by twelve cents — while Hapag-Lloyd fell 3.7 % and FedEx and UPS were down 2 % in the US. Cost: one history pull, one page read, one search.

**The lessons first.**
1. *A pre-announced number landed on predicts "not re-paid", not "sold".* HSBC −5.4, Samsung −4.1, DHL +0.2: the structure is 3 of 3 for no new payment and 2 of 3 for a sell. The two sells had a second negative line the crowd had not priced (HSBC's buyback at the expected size after a hoped-for raise; Samsung's deferred buyback structure); DHL's second line (an FCF miss on IEEPA timing) was explained in the statement and ignored. Rule: on a landed-on pre-announced number the modal close is inside ±1 %, and the DOWN side needs a second unpriced negative line to carry size; without one, no call or the shrink line. Disproof: the next landed-on pre-announced print without a second line closes ≤ −1.5 %.
2. *The n = 1 own analog was given a weight the Nov 4 rule forbids.* The Jul→Aug 2026 full-report-after-pre-release sell (−3.7) carried P(B down) 0.65 in the table; the Nov 4 lesson 4 ("a one-observation habit is not a habit; n ≥ 3 to move a weight by more than 0.10") was read and not followed. Marked **not followed**. The analog is now 1 of 2.
3. *Lesson 8 has inverted on raised-table names.* The unquantified or missed negative line was ignored on LLY (US price −7 %), BMW (the Q4 charge), and DHL (FCF €400 M under the street): 0 of 3 sold when the table had been raised within the quarter. It still sells on met tables (VZ, HSBC) and on cut tables. Restated: the crowd grades the unquantified negative line only when the table has not been raised; after a raise it grades the raise.
4. *The disproof note was the forecast.* Both the DHL doc and the MSFT doc said in advance that a close inside ±0.7 % would mean "already priced" — and that was the modal outcome on both. When the doc's own no-information band is the modal close, the call is a coin and the size should be the shrink line or nothing; the 2.0 was a size on a scenario, not on the modal print.

- **Standing lessons marked (DHL):** 1 met; 2 hurt; 3 n/a; 4 hurt; 5 inverted; 6 met; 7 weak (KS1's FCF clause was already met at lock); 8 inverted; 9 structure governed, sign landed inside the band against me; 10 met. Nov 4 lesson 4 (n ≥ 3) **not followed**.
- **Tallies (D), carried from the Nov 4 evening entry (provisional closes there) plus DHL:** pre-release research **47 of 77** (individually 48 of 84); coin 41.5 of 78. Brier running: 77 scored pre-release entries **18.5125 vs the coin's 19.250** (mean 0.2404 vs 0.250). DOWN calls 39 of 62; UP calls 6 of 15; post-release class 1 of 2. Coin-distance: P(up) ≤ 0.42 **17 of 24** (DHL the eighth miss in the band this fortnight; the Nov 4 lesson 6 question — is the name ≥ 25 % off its high with a met-or-better modal print — did not apply here: at a record, met table; the band's failure case tonight is a landed-on number with no second line).
- **Pending:** QCOM (DOWN 7.0) and ARM (DOWN 6.0) on the Nov 5 US close (21:40 UTC wake); Sony (UP 3.0) and Nintendo (UP 4.0) on the Nov 6 Tokyo close (07:05 UTC wake).

Written 2026-11-05 21:45-22:20 UTC. **QCOM: DOWN 7.0 % at P(up) 0.36, realised −9.59 % (173.19 → 156.58) → HIT (Brier 0.1296), right reason, size by the tape. ARM: DOWN 6.0 % at P(up) 0.42, realised −13.03 % (191.80 → 166.80) → HIT (Brier 0.1764), luck: tape right, table wrong.** Both coins (UP) missed. The day: SPY −4.27 %, QQQ −6.27 %, **SOXX −12.77 %**, SMH −11.50 %, SOXL −38.9 %, NVDA −8.9, AMD −17.4, AVGO −10.2 — the largest semiconductor-index day in this ledger, attributed by the press to the midterm result and AI-regulation fears without an agreed trigger (S). Relative to SOXX: QCOM +3.2 pts (a B print with a named Amazon data-centre line, bought relative), ARM −0.3 pts (an A print — revenue above the range, Dec guide $1.54 B above the $1.50 B line — read as nothing). Cost: one quote pass, four EDGAR exhibits, one fetch agent (the DraftKings release is not yet on EDGAR), no searches for the grades.

**The lessons first.**
1. *On a sector-liquidation day the print does not set the absolute sign; it sets the relative move.* QCOM −9.6 with SOXX −12.8 (print bought +3.2 relative), ARM −13.0 (print neutral). KS6 ("SOXX ≤ −2 % → down regardless, size at the C leg") fired on both and named the sign and the winning size line both times (C legs 10 and 13 against 9.6 and 13.0 realised). Record for the clause: **2 of 2 on sign, 2 of 2 on size** (SNDK and INTC on Oct 30 were the same shape, so 4 of 4 as a pattern). What it cannot do: be known at the lock the evening before. Rule restated: on after-close prints the next-day index term is **unsigned at lock** and must be written as an explicit line with its weight ("tape term, unsigned, 0.30-0.40 of the variance on a ≥ 2 % index day"), not folded into a 5 % scenario D — the Nov 5 documents under-wrote it and the user said so before the close. Disproof: a ≥ 5 % SOXX day on which two graded semiconductor prints close against the index.
2. *The first-scan × 0.4 rule applies only on a calm tape.* QCOM close / scan 1.8× (−5.3 → −9.6), ARM 8.8× (−1.5 → −13.0). The Nov 3 rule carried its exception ("unless a liquidation day") and the exception was the whole story. Record: 7 of 9 digested on calm tapes; 0 of 4 on liquidation days (SNDK, INTC, QCOM, ARM extended). Rule: read the index futures at the grading-day open before applying the 0.4; if the index is ≥ 2 % either way, drop the scan and use the KS6 line.
3. *A ≥ 4 % raise above the street inside the tape (the AMD lesson, 1 of 1 bought) is 1 of 2.* Arm's Dec guide of $1.54 B was 3-6 % above the ≈ $1.45-1.50 B street with revenue above the range and a silicon order book, and the after-hours scan was −1.5 %: the raise was not paid even before the tape opened. The difference from AMD: AMD's raise came with a 2027 growth frame on a flushed base and a rallying index; Arm's came with an 8 % royalty line explained by memory pricing. The rule needs both qualifiers: a raise above the street **and** no decelerating core line (royalty growth for Arm is the cRPO of this cohort). Disproof: the next inside-tape raise with a decelerating core line bought ≥ +3 %.
4. *Inside the tape the street at the top of the company's EPS range is a bar landed on (QCOM, hypothesis 2 confirmed).* EPS $2.17 inside $2.05-2.25 but below the $2.23 street: sold −5.3 % on the scan with the revenue at the high end and QCT above its range. Record: 2 of 2 (Samsung's landed-on operating profit, QCOM). The up-side line (EPS guide midpoint $2.25 vs $2.18 street; Amazon) was paid only relative to the sector.
5. *The lopsided-down own cell after a drawdown inside the tape is 3 of 3 (Samsung, Intel, QCOM), but tonight's observation is contaminated by the tape:* the own-cell sell was the −5.3 % scan; the −9.6 % close was the index. Keep the count at 3 of 3 with the caveat; the next clean test decides whether the cell or the regime is doing the work. The **cleared-lowered-bar inside the tape (ARM, hypothesis 1)** is "untested on a clean tape" rather than 0 of 1: the bar was cleared and not paid in after-hours (−1.5 %), then the index took it.
6. *Size.* Conditional lines (6.3, 6.9) were 3-6 pts short; the C legs (10, 13) were within 0.4 and 0.0. The size rule for after-close inside-tape names now reads: write the C leg as the size **conditional on KS6**, the conditional otherwise, and state both in the CALL line's note so the grade can score the branch that occurred. Record: C leg nearest on 7 of the last 8 inside-tape sold prints (INTC, RDDT, SNDK, COIN, MSTR, DDOG, QCOM, ARM; AMZN the exception at the mixture).
7. *First scans for tonight's three calls (observations, not grades; the Nov 6 close grades them).* **NET +18.9 % after hours** (Q3 $782.5 M, 6.2 % above the guide; cRPO accelerated to 37 %; Q4 guide $845-846 M against the $805 M A line; FY $2,963 M) — the de-rated software rule's qualifier ("every line intact") held at the scan: the four cohort sells (NOW, RDDT, PLTR, DDOG) each had a broken line and Cloudflare had none. **ABNB +11.3 %** (Q3 $4.9 B above the range; **nights +16 % from +10 %**; Q4 guide +16-18 %; FY margin to ≈ 36 %) — the call's FX-lap deceleration mechanism was the wrong line: the Q2 letter had said nights were "accelerating" and I weighted the dollar over the volume line and Expedia's B2B-driven sell over Booking's clean buy. **DKNG −18.3 %** (Q3 $1,329 M below the $1.37-1.42 B street; FY26 EBITDA cut to $175-275 M on a $650-700 M Predictions investment; FY27 EBITDA $800 M-1.0 B given) — the "maintained" modal table at 0.48 was wrong; the August framing ("core ≈ $1 B, guidance $700-900 M") named an investment gap without a number, and the number came in at more than twice the gap. Tentative lesson (n = 1, H): *an investment line framed without a number is raised when the number arrives*; the DOWN side needed the Jefferies cut (a street-lowered bar in the making) weighted as a C term, not 0.25. The three scans have opposite signs on the same tape (+19, +11, −18): at the first scan the tables set the sign, not the index — the test proposed to the user before the close ("all three close with the tape's sign") is failing at the scan and will be read at the close.

- **Standing lessons marked (QCOM / ARM):** 1 met ×2; 2 helped ×2; 3 helped / inverted in substance (a beaten table sold by the tape); 4 helped / n/a; 5 consistent (extended on a liquidation day) / inverted; 6 **not met ×2** (closes are the Nasdaq 4 pm official prints; settled rows to confirm Nov 6); 7 met ×2 (both street figures were S and held); 8 helped ×2; 9 regime hit ×2 (for the sector's reason on ARM); 10 met ×2 (coins missed).
