## 2026-11-25 (method) — checking a viral pattern: a 5 % fall, a 5 % rise, then a gap down, on the Nasdaq 100 fund

Written while checking a post the user sent, no call locked. The lessons file was not printed again (last full read 18:31 UTC Nov 24; receipt lapsed on the entries added since). Daily open and close of the Nasdaq 100 fund from one vendor, Mar 1999 to Nov 24 2026; closes are not adjusted for dividends.

- **The post's list is correct.** A close 5 % or more lower, then a close 5 % or more higher, then an open 1 % or more lower: Jan 4 2001, Mar 14 2001, Oct 17 2008, Mar 11, 16 and 18 2020, and Nov 24 2026. Further fall to the lowest later close: 67.9 %, 54.9 %, 20.8 %, 17.1 %, 12.0 %, 6.4 %.
- **Six cases are three episodes.** The three in 2020 fall within six sessions of each other.
- **What the post left out.** A year later the fund was higher in 4 of 6 (minus 34, minus 16, plus 33, plus 52, plus 66, plus 77 %). After 63 sessions, 4 of 6.
- **What held in all six.** A further fall of 6 % or more before the low, and a lower close five sessions on in 5 of 6.
- **Where they started.** The 2001 and 2008 cases began 41 to 62 % under the 252-session high; the 2020 cases 14 to 23 % under; Nov 24 2026 began 14.3 % under.
- **The gap filter does the selecting.** Without it there are 11 earlier cases, five of them in 2000, and the first, in January 2000, was followed by plus 18.5 % in 63 sessions. With thresholds loosened to 4.5 %, 4.5 % and 0.8 %, August 2011 and November 2008 join.
- **Rule:** a list of matching dates is checked for three things before it is given weight: how many separate episodes, the outcome at more than one horizon, and whether the list survives a small change in its thresholds.
- **Disproof of the one thing that held:** the fund not closing 6 % under its Nov 23 2026 close (651.03) before making a new 252-session high.

## 2026-11-25 (method, second note) — what followed a first Nasdaq close 20 % under a peak, by horizon and by speed

Written while answering a question, no call locked. Nasdaq Composite daily closes from one vendor, Feb 1971 to Nov 24 2026. An episode starts at the first close 10 % under the highest close since the last episode ended and ends at a new high or a 20 % rise off its low; the low is the episode's own low, so the legs of 2000 to 2002 and of 2022 count separately. Definitions fixed before the counts were read. Receipt valid (69c3c601e72b).

- **Twenty first closes at 20 %.** Higher after 5 sessions in 12, after 21 in 10, after 63 in 13, after 126 in 13, after 252 in 12 (median plus 8.1 %, worst minus 49.3 %, best plus 85.0 %). Controls from any day: 58, 61, 66, 70 and 77 %. The label lowers the one-year odds from 77 % to 60 % and widens the range.
- **Further fall to the episode low:** under 5 % in 7, 5 to 15 % in 4, over 15 % in 9; median 13.6 %. Sessions to the low: 10 or fewer in 7, 11 to 40 in 5, more than 40 in 8.
- **Speed splits them.** Reached within 45 sessions of the peak (9 cases): further fall under 5 % in 5, median 4.7 %; low within 10 sessions in 5; higher a year on in 7 of 9, median plus 30.2 %. Slower (11 cases): median further fall 17.0 %; higher a year on in 5 of 11, median minus 8.6 %.
- **Six of the nine fast ones started from a record close** (1978, 1980, 1987, 1998, April 2000, 2020): further fall 0.2, 0.0, 19.0, 5.3, 16.1 and 4.7 %; higher a year on in 5 of 6. The exception is April 2000, minus 48 %.
- **The first month is a coin either way:** 10 of 20 higher at 21 sessions.
- **A check on a post:** the volatility index closed at 30 or more on the Wednesday before Thanksgiving twice since 1990 (2008: 54.92; 2011: 33.98). It was 20.35 in 2022. After 2008 the S&P 500 lost 21.1 % in 63 sessions; after 2011 it gained 17.7 %. On all 736 days with a close of 30 or more the index was still 25 or more five sessions later 96 % of the time, so the holiday adds nothing.
- **Rule:** when a label has a fast kind and a slow kind, say which kind today is before quoting the pooled figure.
- **Disproof:** the next two fast cases from a record both lower a year on.

## 2026-11-25 (US) — Dell, Ambarella, HP, Workday and Autodesk (Nov 25 close vs Nov 24; after-close reports Nov 24); Nov 25 closes provisional on one vendor's rows

Written 2026-11-25 22:00 UTC. **What was read of LESSONS.md before writing: not printed again for this grade. All 15 parts were printed in full ending 18:31 UTC on Nov 24 in this same context, the entries added on Nov 24 and 25 were printed uncut with the changes command at 11:01 UTC today, and one method note was written after that. The guard reports the receipt lapsed on its 24-hour limit; a full read is owed before the next prediction.** **Dell: DOWN 3.0 % at P(up) 0.48 -> plus 0.60 % (295.28 -> 297.06), MISS; residual minus 1.35 against plus 3.0 called, MISS; coin UP hit; Brier 0.2704. Ambarella: DOWN 6.0 % at 0.36 -> minus 19.77 % (44.47 -> 35.68), HIT, right reason; coin UP missed; Brier 0.1296. HP: DOWN 3.5 % at 0.41 -> plus 11.49 % (27.94 -> 31.15), MISS, fundamentals miss; coin DOWN missed; Brier 0.3481. Workday: DOWN 3.0 % at 0.45 -> plus 0.68 % (179.23 -> 180.44), MISS; coin UP hit; Brier 0.3025. Autodesk: DOWN 2.5 % at 0.47 -> minus 3.49 % (255.70 -> 246.79), HIT on the sheet, luck by the rubric; coin DOWN hit; Brier 0.2209.** Two of five; the coin three of five. Market line: S&P 500 UP 0.3 % called, minus 0.25 % realised (7,201.09 -> 7,182.78), MISS. Tape: Nasdaq Composite minus 0.62 % (23,585.80) after trading 4.6 % lower; chip fund minus 0.50 %; software fund minus 0.35 %. Cost: one price pull, five exhibits, no searches; no call was read. Dell's and Workday's signs rest on 0.6 and 0.7 points of provisional closes.

**The lessons first.**
1. *I shorted a company-lowered bar, the one case my own ban let me call long.* HP had said PC units would fall and memory costs would rise. It then guided fiscal 2027 EPS to $3.15-3.45 and gained 11.5 %. This file has that pattern bought every time: Novartis, Merck, AstraZeneca, Novo, BMW, McDonald's, Sony on the relative line, and now HP, eight of eight. The Oct 27 entry says in its first lesson that a pre-announced negative is a lowered bar and not the thing the crowd will sell. I read HP's Sep 21 statement as the source of a cut line. **Rule: a negative the company has itself stated before the report cannot be the reason for a short. It lowers the bar. A short needs a negative the company has not flagged, or one it says has got worse since.** Ambarella is the second half: its memory constraint was flagged and then "intensified", and it lost 20 %. Count: flagged negative, no worsening stated, bought 8 of 8; flagged and stated worse, sold 1 of 1. Disproof: two of the next four flagged-negative reports closing down 2 % or more without the company saying it worsened.
2. *The named-cut-line rule is finished as a way to pick shorts.* Named mechanisms have now happened 1 time in 9 (Home Depot; not Siemens, Walmart, Intuit, Zoom, Symbotic, HP, Workday, Autodesk). Shorts made on them outside the AI tape: 4 of 9. Its Nov 10 disproof, three of six missing while the print is a raise, is met without stretching: Siemens, Zoom, HP, Workday. Yesterday's narrower version, a cut line resting on a quantified item in the company's own report, failed on its first use: HP's $0.19 of refunds was such an item. **Rule from the next lock: outside the AI tape I do not short on a cut line I have built. The short side there needs one of three counted cells: an own habit of 7 of 8 down, a flagged negative the company says has worsened, or a name that has held up while its sector fell (Verizon, Autodesk: 2 of 2). Otherwise the reply says no call, or long on a lowered bar if the user allows it.** Disproof: three of the next five shorts made under those three cells missing.
3. *Forced shorts at a coin are 2 of 5 and their coins are 5 of 5.* Zoom, Dell and Workday lost; Semtech and Autodesk won, one on the tape and one by luck on the rubric. A side forced by a ban on the other side is not a forecast. I told the user so each time and logged it as a call anyway. **Rule proposed, for the user to rule on with the long type: within 0.05 of a coin and with no counted cell on either side, the reply says no call and nothing goes on the sheet.**
4. *An own habit at threshold held inside the AI tape again.* Ambarella: lower after seven of eight reports now. Own lopsided-down cells after a drawdown inside the tape: Samsung, Intel, Qualcomm, Ambarella, four of four. It is the only cell that gave a clean hit this round.
5. *Dell opened 9.4 % lower on a raise of $10 billion and closed 0.6 % higher.* First reactions mis-stated the close: 12 of 12. A raise from 25 % or more under the high, off liquidation days: 6 of 8. The ban on calls to rise inside the tape has its first exception since it was written: scenario A prints there while the chip fund is under its 50-day average are 1 up of 2 (Semtech down, Dell up); its disproof needs two of three.
6. *The residual line is no better than the absolute one.* Dell's was wrong in sign; Ambarella's was right in sign and 17 points short. Residual calls right in sign: 4 of 8. I stop calling it "the forecast" inside the tape; it is a second coin until it is better than 6 of 10.
7. *The index reading I gave the user at 16:02 UTC was 2.8 % lower and the close was 0.25 % lower.* The Nasdaq 100 touched its 20 % line to the point and closed well above it. I labelled the reading mid-session and it still shaped my reply about "more downside". The pre-holiday cell (58 of 76) missed and is 58 of 77.
8. *Size.* Ambarella: 6.0 called, 19.8 realised, C leg 15. The rule that a C print sizes at the C leg held; I had sized to the shrink line because the stock had already lost 20 % the day before. After a one-day fall of that size the shrink rule (written for a five-session fall of 15 %) did not apply and I half-applied it.
9. *A third raise sold (Autodesk) is a hit I cannot explain.* Three of three raises sold on this name. The line I named was nudged up. It goes in the tally and into the "held up while its sector fell" cell only, because that condition was checkable before the report.

- **Standing lessons marked (DELL / AMBA / HPQ / WDAY / ADSK):** cost met x5; crowd digestion: coin / helped / hurt / coin / helped without the mechanism; fitting cell: the ban, lost by 0.6 / own habit, helped / first-time-guide cut line, hurt / sector overhang, lost by 0.7 (now 3 of 5 with Autodesk) / overhang on a name that held up, helped; counted up line: lowered to 0.60, fired / 0.60, did not fire / lowered to 0.35 on the company's own statement, fired / lowered to 0.40, fired / lowered to 0.50, fired (lowered up lines fired 10 of 14: the lowering is wrong seven times in ten); P(B) cap: the middle case printed in none of five; lowered bar cleared is bought: not followed on HP; my alternative was the forecast: HP, count 6; scenario letter predicts the close outside the tape on calm days: HP A up, Workday A up, Autodesk A down, 12 of 14; raised or beaten table outside the tape bought: 20 of 25; residual: wrong / right sign; kill line: fired and told the truth on Dell, HP, Workday; fired on a hit on Autodesk; extra-week search: done on all five releases, none found; index term: unsigned at lock, minus 0.25 %, decided nothing; open calls reconciled: written; row 11: no call read x5.
- **Tallies (D), carried from the Nov 24 (US) entry plus these five:** pre-release research **60.0 of 110**; coin 56.5 of 111. Brier running: 110 scored entries **26.8286 vs the coin's 27.500** (mean 0.2439 vs 0.250). DOWN calls **50 of 83**; UP calls 7 of 25; post-release class 1 of 3; TAPE class 4 of 4 on sign. ## 2026-11-25 (method, third note) — the user's ruling: a side every time; and what I had not been looking at

Written 2026-11-25 about 22:20 UTC after the user answered my two questions. No call locked. The file was not printed again; the receipt has lapsed on its 24-hour limit.

**The ruling (the user's words, in substance).** "No call" is not allowed: the price closes up or down, so I give a side every time. Where I am at a coin the fix is to widen what I look at, not to abstain. The user did not rule in words on the ban on long calls. My reading, labelled as mine: a side forced by a ban is the luck the user is telling me to stop relying on, so from the next lock the ban no longer picks the side. The side comes from the counted cell that fits, and where none fits, from the two measurements below. If the user meant otherwise they will say so.

**What my own ledger says (D, from CALIBRATION.csv, 127 graded rows counted one by one).** My side was right 68 times, 54 %. Calling every one of them short would have been right 77 times, 61 %. Through October: mine 43 of 69, always-short 44 of 69. Nov 1 to 18: mine 19 of 45, always-short 25. Since Nov 19: mine 6 of 13, always-short 8. In no period did my choice of side beat one fixed side. The research has read the reports well and has added nothing on direction that a single line would not have given. That is the measured starting point, and it is worse than the tallies made it look.

**Two things I never measured before a lock, both reachable today (M).**
1. *The street's number for the line being guided, and how it has been revised.* The exchange's own site serves, per company, the consensus EPS for each coming quarter and year with the low, the high, the count of estimates and the revisions up and down over four weeks (`api.nasdaq.com/api/analyst/<TICKER>/earnings-forecast`). Read today, a day after the reports and showing no sign of having been updated: HP fiscal 2027 at $3.06 (range $2.75 to $3.36, six estimates, one cut and no raise in four weeks). My cut line was "under $3.00". So my thesis sat two per cent under a number the street already held, and the guide of $3.15 to $3.45 beat that number by eight per cent at its midpoint. For three of this week's calls I wrote "the street's figure was not found". It was one request away. The basis differs by company (HP's is the adjusted figure; Workday's and Autodesk's look like GAAP), so the basis is checked against the company's last reported figure before use.
2. *The option market's price for the move.* The same site serves the option chain (`api.nasdaq.com/api/quote/<TICKER>/option-chain`). The nearest expiry's at-the-money call plus put, over the price, is the move the market expects; the gap between a put and a call the same distance out is its lean. I have written "option chain not read" in nearly every document since October.

**Rules from the next lock.**
- Every lock records, in the table of facts: the street's figure for each line the company will guide, its low and high, the revisions up and down over four weeks, and the option-implied move with its lean. A document that says "not found" for the street's forward figure names the request that failed.
- A cut line or a raise line of mine is written against the street's figure, not against the company's last year. If my line is within three per cent of the street's, I have no view on that line and it cannot pick the side.
- Cuts to the street's figure in the four weeks before a report are counted as a lowered bar, with the count stated.
- These two measurements are new and have no record. Each is scored at every grade (did the side they pointed to win) and gets no more weight than a counted cell until it has ten cases.
- **Disproof:** after twenty locks with both recorded, the side they point to wins no more often than always-short did over the same twenty.

**What stays unknowable at the lock.** The index's move on the grading day. It took the absolute sign on every day it moved three per cent or more. No search fixes that; the reply will keep saying so in one line.

Earlier lessons that applied. No call to rise inside the AI trade while the chip fund is under its 50-day average: hurt; scenario A prints there are now 1 up of 2. Lowering the counted odds of a raise without the company's own statement: hurt again, lowered lines fired 10 of 14. A first reaction is not the result: held, the stock opened 9.4 % lower and closed higher, 12 of 12.

Descriptions, not lessons. Revenue $54.9 B; year raised to $202 B; AI-server backlog $132.3 B; a $25 B buyback added. ## Trust calibration (standing section; updated at every grading)

The asset is not the hit rate, it is the record of *which kind of call* can be trusted and by how much. `CALIBRATION.csv` holds one row per call (side, size, P(up), realised move, hit/miss); this section states the rule that reads it. A future session starts from this section and the standing lessons, not from pretraining priors.

- **Call types and their graded record (as of 2026-10-22, 34 graded incl. the post-release class):**
  - DOWN on a good print, **inside the AI-capex tape** (semis, equipment, hyperscalers, AI-power, storage, Tesla): **18 of 20** hits (misses: ASML day one, PEP-type; BE and WDC added Oct 23). Trust: high, as long as the AI-spend doubt holds (disproof: two AI-capex A prints bought in five).
  - DOWN on a good print **outside the AI-capex tape**: the reset triggered Oct 22 (SPCX, IBM, LMT bought); AXP sold Oct 23 by its own habit; VZ sold Oct 26 by a dated sector overhang (SpaceX) despite a 7-of-8 up habit. Trust: untested as a regime; **the name's own print record governs when it is ≥ 7 of 8 one way and no dated sector overhang is in force** (own-habit rule 1 of 2); with an overhang, the overhang takes the side. Score raises across the whole guide table (LMT) and check the unquantified consensus line (VZ total revenue). European names: own cell governs (ASML, HSBC, SHEL hits; NOVN and VOW3 misses, both cells overriding a de-rated base) — **amended Oct 29: unless the base is ≥ 25 % below the high and the cell is not lopsided, in which case the base classification (UP) governs, sized at the shrink line.** The threshold-habit rule (≥ 7 of 8 one way governs over the base) is 3 of 4 (AXP, HOOD, SHEL hits; VZ lost to a dated overhang). Passing a pre-announced bar is bought (hynix, SHEL, XOM); landing on it is not re-paid — sold when a second unpriced negative line is present (HSBC, Samsung), flat without one (DHL +0.2, Nov 5): the modal close is inside ±1 %, so the DOWN side needs that second line or the shrink line. A pre-announced negative line (cost step-up) is a lowered bar; clearing it is up-side information (NOVN). A buyback at the expected size is a met pre-released line, zero information (HSBC).
  - DOWN calls overall 23 of 31 pre-release (as of 2026-10-27 evening). Outside-tape record since the Oct 22 reset: bought — SPCX, IBM, LMT, SPOT, PYPL, UPS (de-rated bases with raised guide tables); sold — AXP (1-of-9 habit), VZ (dated overhang), KO (defensive, sector day), BA (quantified-line misses). Rule (superseded 2026-10-29 evening): outside the tape, de-rated + raised table → UP; chased (within 10 % of the 52-week high or +15 % over 21 sessions) → DOWN; own habit overrides only at ≥ 7 of 8; a dated overhang takes the side; defensives at highs are a coin, sized small, never UP. The skew tie-break is retired (0 of 2). **Rule in force from 2026-10-29 evening (amended Oct 30: the de-rated-base UP applies only when every line of the table is intact — a live user/volume/guide C-line outranks the base, RDDT −29 %; and on names whose print-day sign follows the sector ≥ 9 of 10, a sector bid ≥ 1.5 % pre-market at lock takes the side or there is no call, CVX/XOM): outside the tape the table governs, not the base — raised or beaten above the whole range → UP whatever the base (13 of 13 since Oct 22: SPCX, IBM, LMT, SPOT, PYPL, UPS, SHEL, CMG, EBAY, FDX, MA, MRK, LLY); met or landed-on → base and own habit decide, sized small; cut → DOWN. The chased-base rule is retired (0 of 3 on raised tables: EBAY, MA, LLY). A street-lowered bar (≥ 5 cuts in 30 days, stock sold into the print) cleared with the FY raised is an UP configuration (NOVN, MRK: 2 of 2 as a pattern, 0 of 2 as calls). Two-observation seasonal cells (Octobers 0 of 2) are retired, 0 of 4.** Inside the tape 19 of 22 good prints sold, the sell shrinking (−11, −3, −1) and then the first bought: SK hynix +10.9 on Oct 28, 55 % below its high after a 21 % index crash with the tape bouncing. **Amendment (2026-10-28, revised that evening): the disproof condition (two A prints bought in five: hynix, STX) is met — the DOWN-on-good-print type is reset to untested. By cell: lopsided own cells (≥ 7 of 9) govern after a ≥ 25 % drawdown (hynix, STX bought); no-lean cells keep the regime's shape, sold and small (GEV −1.9, GLW −1.1); at or near highs the regime governs until a print at the highs is bought (MSFT/GOOGL on Oct 29 are the test). In a bouncing tape size at the mixture median or shrink line: the day-one sell now happens at the open and is bought back by the close.** Size off the live daily sigma when it is more than twice the historical. A pre-announced number is a bar only when the company lands on it (hynix: ₩16 T rumoured, ₩100 T delivered). - **Regime statement (2026-10-22):** the sold-print regime is an AI-capex-tape phenomenon. Inside it, raises are sold whatever they contain (ASML, TSMC, GE, Meta, Lam, TI, Tesla); outside it, good prints from de-rated or chased bases are bought (SpaceX, SAP, IBM, Lockheed, Spotify). Classify the name first, then pick the type.
- **Process, enforced by a hook since 2026-11-10:** a new call document cannot be committed unless this file was printed in full into the current conversation (`.claude/hooks/lessons_guard.py`, wired in `.claude/settings.json`); the receipt dies on compaction, a fresh session, a change to this file, or 24 hours, and its line goes in the call's lock line. Written after two locks in two days were made on partial or day-old reads.
- **How trust moves:** each type's trust is its graded record; a type with ≥ 8 graded calls and ≥ 70 % hits may size at the conditional; a type under 60 % or under 5 calls sizes at the shrink line regardless of conviction. A regime flip (two bought A prints in five) resets the DOWN-on-good-print type to "untested" and the UP type to "watch".
- **Second-print and label rules (from SPCX, 2026-10-21):** a new issuer's second print is graded on whether the first print's fear is answered on the revenue line, not on the first reaction's sign; a first GAAP profit on a loss-making base is a bought headline. Record 0 of 1 for the naive repeat.
- **What a future session must not do:** trust pretraining ("beats get bought") over this record — with the 2026-10-29 qualification that outside the AI tape the record now agrees with it (13 of 13 raised tables bought) and inside the tape it does not (25 of 30 good prints sold), so the classification is the work; spend agents where a data pull suffices; lock before reading the release clock and the company's own filings; grade without marking every standing lesson.
